\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[2\bigg\{\frac{C\varepsilon^{2(p-2)}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}S}\bigg[\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{r}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N+2}}\bigg\}^{\frac{N+2}{2(r+4-p)}}\bm{b}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-1}\Bigg]^{\frac{2(r+4-p)}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2(2-p)(N+2)}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}}\frac{1}{[(1-\sigma)^{2}S]^{\frac{N+2}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}}\bigg[\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2(2-p)(N+2)}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}}\frac{\big(R^{2}/S\big)^{\frac{N}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}}{(1-\sigma)^{\frac{2(N+2)}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{\bm{\nu}_{r}}}.

Joining this with the first alternative, we obtain the claimed quantitative
L^{\infty}
-gradient bound.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

The preceding gradient bound corresponds to
[
17
, Chapter VIII, Theorem 5.2]
. To allow
r=p
, we need
\bm{\nu}_{p}=N(p-2)+2p>0
which is equivalent to
p>\frac{2N}{N+2}
. In this case the gradient bound simplifies to

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}(z_{o})}|Du|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max\Bigg\{\Bigg[\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2(2-p)(N+2)}{2}}}\frac{\big(R^{2}/S\big)^{\frac{N}{2}}}{(1-\sigma)^{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{d}{p}},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{S}{R^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2-p}},\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\},

where

d:=\frac{2p}{(N+2)p-2N}

stands for the
scaling deficit
.
Note that
d
blows up in the limit
p\downarrow\frac{2N}{N+2}
.

Remark 3.7
.

A careful examination of the proof shows that when
p=2
, we can take
\varepsilon=1
and, quite obviously,
\mu=0
. Moreover, due to the natural homogeneity of the equation, we can also assume
S=R^{2}
. Hence, the scaling deficit
d\equiv 1
, and the previous estimate reduces to

\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma R^{2}}(z_{o})}|Du|\leq\Bigg[\frac{C}{(1-\sigma)^{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,R^{2}}(z_{o})}|Du|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

When
p\in(1,\frac{2N}{N+2}]
, a gradient bound cannot be estimated via the
L^{p}
-norm of
Du
in general like in Remark
3.6
. However, it is possible if we consider bounded solutions.

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
\mu\in(0,1]
and
1<p<2
.
Then, for any locally bounded weak solution
u
to the parabolic system (
3.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
with assumption (
3.2
), we have
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E_{T})
.
Moreover, there exist positive constants
\theta=\theta(N,p)
and
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},C_{2})
such that for every cylinder
Q_{R,S}(z_{o})\Subset E_{T}
with
0<R\leq 1
and
S>0
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\frac{1}{2}Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bm{\mathfrak{T}}^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R,2S}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}},

(3.21)

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\max\Bigg\{\,}\varepsilon\Big(\frac{S}{R^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2-p}},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\mu^{\frac{N(2-p)+4p}{4p}},\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\},

for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, where

\bm{\mathfrak{T}}:=\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\sqrt{S}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}},

and

\bm{\omega}:=\sup_{t\in(t_{o}-2S,t_{o}]}\sup_{x\in B_{2R}(x_{o})}\big|u(x,t)-(u)_{x_{o},2R}(t)\big|.

Proof..

In the gradient bound from Proposition
3.5
, we rename
r
by
m
and fix
\sigma=\frac{1}{2}
. Moreover, we take
m:=\frac{N(2-p)}{2}+2p
so that
\bm{\nu}_{m}=4p>0
.
The abbreviation
m
is retained at various points in the following calculation to keep some exponents short.
For the particular choice of
m
we have
m>\max\big\{\frac{N(2-p)}{2},p+1\big\}
.
Moreover, since
N\geq 2
, we also have
m\geq 2+p>3
.
With these choices we get from Proposition
3.5
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\frac{1}{2}Q_{R,S}}

\displaystyle|Du|

(3.22)

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{\frac{C_{\infty}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{(2-p)(N+2)}{2p}}}\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{m}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{S}{R^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2-p}},\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\}.

The integral in the first entry of the maximum can be estimated by means of the
L^{m}
-estimate (
3.3
) from Lemma
3.3
. In fact, we have

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{m}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\leq C_{m}\max\Bigg\{\frac{\bm{\mathfrak{T}}^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}}{\delta^{\frac{(2-p)(m-p)}{2p^{2}}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R,2S}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}},(\delta\mu)^{\frac{m}{2p}}\Bigg\}

for every
\delta\in(0,1]
.
Now, we distinguish between two cases, depending on whether the maximum is assumed by the former or latter entry. If the second entry is larger,
we choose
\delta
in terms of
\varepsilon
such that

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\infty}C_{m}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{(2-p)(N+2)}{2p}}}\,\delta^{\frac{m}{2p}}=\varepsilon\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\delta=\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{N(2-p)+4}{m}}}{[C_{\infty}C_{m}]^{\frac{2p}{m}}}.

This specifies the value of
\delta

and leads to the estimate

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\infty}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{(2-p)(N+2)}{2p}}}

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{m}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\infty}C_{m}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{(2-p)(N+2)}{2p}}}\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\delta^{\frac{m}{2p}}\mu^{\frac{m}{2p}}\leq\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\mu^{\frac{N(2-p)+4p}{4p}}.

If the first entry is larger,
for the same value of
\delta
we have

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\infty}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{(2-p)(N+2)}{2p}}}

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{m}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\infty}C_{m}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{(2-p)(N+2)}{2p}}\delta^{\frac{(2-p)(m-p)}{2p^{2}}}}\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bm{\mathfrak{T}}^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R,2S}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle=\frac{[C_{\infty}C_{m}]^{1+\frac{(2-p)(m-p)}{mp}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bm{\mathfrak{T}}^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R,2S}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bm{\mathfrak{T}}^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R,2S}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}.

Here,
\theta:=\frac{(2-p)}{2p^{2}}[p(N+2)+\frac{(m-p)(N(2-p)+4)}{m}]
effectively depends on
N
and
p
.
Combining the two cases with
(
3.4
) we get (
3.8
). This finishes the proof.
∎

Taking a more special geometry of cylinders, the previous result yields a gradient bound that will be useful in later applications.

Corollary 3.9
.

Let
\mu\in(0,1]
,
1<p\leq 2
, and
\lambda\geq\mu
. Then, for any locally bounded weak solution
u
to the parabolic system (
3.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
with assumption (
3.2
), we have
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E_{T})
.
Moreover, there exist positive constants
\theta=\theta(N,p)
and
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},C_{2})
such that for every cylinder
Q_{2R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\Subset E_{T}
with
0<R\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\frac{1}{2}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{\frac{C\sqrt{\lambda}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\lambda R}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\lambda R}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}},\varepsilon\lambda,\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\}.

where

\bm{\omega}:=\sup_{t\in(t_{o}-\lambda^{2-p}(2R)^{2},t_{o}]}\,\sup_{x\in B_{2R}(x_{o})}\big|u(x,t)-(u)_{x_{o},2R}(t)\big|.

Proof..

Recalling the definition of
\bm{\mathfrak{T}}
from Proposition
3.8
we have

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bm{\mathfrak{T}}^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\sqrt{S}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\frac{N}{4p}(p-2)}\lambda^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\lambda R}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\lambda R}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\lambda}\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\lambda R}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\lambda R}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}.

Moreover, we have
(S/R^{2})^{\frac{1}{2-p}}=\lambda
, and

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}}\mu^{\frac{m}{2p}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\frac{N}{4p}(p-2)}\mu^{\frac{N}{4p}(2-p)+1}=\Big(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\Big)^{\frac{N}{4p}(2-p)}\mu\leq\mu.

Inserting this into (
3.8
), we obtain the claim.
∎

3.5.
Qualitative gradient bound in the super-quadratic case

In the super-quadratic case
p\geq 2
the assumption of local boundedness is superfluous for weak solutions, since they are automatically locally bounded. This has consequences for the
L^{\infty}
-gradient bound. We start with the qualitative bound, which is stated as follows.

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
\mu\in(0,1]
and
p\geq 2
. Then, for any weak solution
u
to the parabolic system (
3.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
with assumption (
3.2
), we have

|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E_{T}).

Proof..

As in the case
1<p\leq 2
the starting point is the energy estimate from Lemma
3.2
in the form (
3.3
). Since now
p\geq 2
, the term with exponent
\frac{p}{2}+\alpha
is dominant on the right-hand side, whereas the other term is controlled by the elementary estimate

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}+\alpha}+1.

Using this inequality in (
3.3
) we obtain

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{1+\alpha}\sup_{t\in(-r^{2},0]}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}\times\{t\}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{1+\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}x+\iint_{Q_{r}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}+\alpha}|D^{2}u|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{C(1+\alpha)}{(R-r)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{R}}\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}+\alpha}+1\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

for any
0<r<R
such that
Q_{R}\Subset E_{T}
.
As in the sub-quadratic case introduce

w=\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p+2\alpha}{4}}

and rewrite the above estimate as

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{1+\alpha}\sup_{t\in(-r^{2},0]}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}\times\{t\}}w^{\frac{4+4\alpha}{p+2\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\tfrac{1}{(p+2\alpha)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{r}}|\nabla w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(3.23)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(1+\alpha)}{(R-r)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{R}}\big[w^{2}+1\big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

The symbolism
\varrho_{i}
,
\tilde{\varrho}_{i}
,
B_{i}
,
Q_{i}
,
\widetilde{B}_{i}
,
\widetilde{Q}_{i}
, and
\kappa
from (
3.13
) is retained. However, the value of
\alpha_{o}
, the recursion formula for
\alpha_{i}
, the definition of
p_{i}
and
q_{i}
, must be adapted to the case
p\geq 2
. To this end, we define

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\alpha_{o}=0,\quad\alpha_{i+1}=\kappa\alpha_{i}+\frac{2}{N},\\[6.0pt]
p_{i}=p+2\alpha_{i},\quad\displaystyle q_{i}=\frac{4+4\alpha_{i}}{p+2\alpha_{i}},\end{array}\right.

for
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
The definition of
\alpha_{i}
immediately yields
\alpha_{i}=\kappa^{i}-1
. Moreover, we have (
3.14
) at hand, namely,
p_{i+1}=(p+2\alpha_{i})\tfrac{N+q_{i}}{N}
. To continue, we choose the standard cut-off function
\zeta\equiv\zeta_{i}\in C^{\infty}(\widetilde{Q}_{i},[0,1])
such that
\zeta_{i}

vanishes on the parabolic boundary
\partial_{\mathrm{par}}\widetilde{Q}_{i}
,
\zeta_{i}
is identically equal to one on
Q_{i+1}
, and the gradient satisfies
|\nabla\zeta_{i}|\leq 2^{i+4}/R
.
An application of the Sobolev embedding
[
17
, Chapter I, Proposition 3.1]
with
p=2
and
m=q_{i}
gives that

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i+1}}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}(w\zeta_{i})^{2\frac{N+q_{i}}{N}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm Sob}(N,q_{i})\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}|\nabla(w\zeta_{i})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg[\sup_{\tau\in(-\tilde{\varrho}_{i}^{2},0]}\int_{\widetilde{B}_{i}\times\{\tau\}}(w\zeta_{i})^{q_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N}}

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}\Big[\zeta_{i}^{2}|\nabla w|^{2}+w^{2}|\nabla\zeta_{i}|^{2}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg[\sup_{\tau\in(-\tilde{\varrho}_{i}^{2},0]}\int_{\widetilde{B}_{i}\times\{\tau\}}w^{q_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N}}

(3.24)

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}\bigg[|\nabla w|^{2}+\frac{2^{2i+8}w^{2}}{R^{2}}\bigg]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg[\sup_{\tau\in(-\tilde{\varrho}_{i}^{2},0]}\int_{\widetilde{B}_{i}\times\{\tau\}}w^{q_{i}}\mathrm{d}x\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N}}

for a constant
C=C(N,p)
.
In the application of the Sobolev inequality, as indicated, there is a dependence of the constant on
q_{i}
. However,
q_{i}
only takes values in the interval
[\frac{4}{p},2]
. Therefore, the continuous dependence of the Sobolev constant on
q_{i}
eventually yields a dependence on
p
. To estimate the right-hand side integrals in the last displayed inequality, we use the energy estimate (
3.5
) applied on
\widetilde{Q}_{i}\subset Q_{i}
. In fact, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}|\nabla w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{(p+2\alpha_{i})^{3}2^{2i+6}}{R^{2}}\iint_{Q_{i}}\big[w^{2}+1\big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(-\tilde{\varrho}_{i}^{2},0]}\int_{\widetilde{B}_{i}\times\{t\}}w^{q_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{(p+2\alpha_{i})^{2}2^{2i+6}}{R^{2}}\iint_{Q_{i}}\big[w^{2}+1\big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Inserting this into (
3.5
) yields

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i+1}}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p+2\alpha_{i})^{3+\frac{4}{N}}\Bigg[\frac{2^{2i}}{R^{2}}\iint_{Q_{i}}\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i}}{2}}+1\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\kappa}.

With the help of

\displaystyle(p+2\alpha_{i})^{3+\frac{4}{N}}2^{2i\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq\big[p(1+\alpha_{i})\big]^{1+2\kappa}2^{2i\kappa}=\big[p\kappa^{i}\big]^{1+2\kappa}2^{2i\kappa}\leq p^{1+2\kappa}(4\kappa^{3})^{i\kappa},

this can be simplified, i.e. we have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{i+1}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i+1}}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Bigg[\frac{\bm{b}^{i}}{R^{2}}\iint_{Q_{i}}\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i}}{2}}+1\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\kappa}

with
\bm{b}=\bm{b}(N)=4\kappa^{3}
and
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},C_{2})
. Divide both sides by
|Q_{i+1}|
to get

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i+1}}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\bm{b}^{i\kappa}\underbrace{\frac{|Q_{i}|^{\kappa}}{R^{2\kappa}|Q_{i+1}|}}_{\leq 2^{N+2}}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{i}}\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i}}{2}}+1\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq C\bm{b}^{i\kappa}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{i}}\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i}}{2}}+1\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\kappa}.

Therefore, adding
1
to the left-hand side we get

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{i+1}^{p_{i+1}}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{i+1}}\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i+1}}{2}}+1\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\bm{b}^{i\kappa}\Bigg[\underbrace{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{i}}\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p_{i}}{2}}+1\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}_{=\bm{Y}_{i}^{p_{i}}}\Bigg]^{\kappa}\equiv\big(C\bm{b}^{i}\bm{Y}_{i}^{p_{i}}\big)^{\kappa}.

Iterating this inequality yields

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{i}^{p_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{i}C^{\kappa^{i-j+1}}\prod_{j=1}^{i}\bm{b}^{j\kappa^{i-j+1}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{p\kappa^{i}},

so that

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[\prod_{j=1}^{i}C^{\frac{\kappa^{i-j+1}}{2(\kappa^{i}-1)}}\Bigg]^{\frac{2(\kappa^{i}-1)}{p_{i}}}\Bigg[\prod_{j=1}^{i}\bm{b}^{\frac{j\kappa^{i-j+1}}{2(\kappa^{i}-1)}}\Bigg]^{\frac{2(\kappa^{i}-1)}{p_{i}}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{\frac{p\kappa^{i}}{p_{i}}}.

Now, Lemma
2.1
, and

\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{2(\kappa^{i}-1)}{p_{i}}=1\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{p\kappa^{i}}{p_{i}}=\frac{p}{2}

allow us to pass to the limit
i\to\infty
in the preceding inequality to conclude that

\displaystyle\limsup_{i\to\infty}\bm{Y}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\frac{N+2}{4}}\bm{b}^{\frac{(N+2)^{2}}{8}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{\frac{p}{2}}.

This immediately implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\frac{1}{2}Q_{R}}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}}\big[|Du|^{p}+1\big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

for a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},C_{2})
. A covering argument then shows
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(E_{T})
. This proves the claim.
∎

3.6.
Quantitative gradient bound in the super-quadratic case

In this section we derive an improved quantitative
L^{\infty}
-gradient bound for weak solutions to parabolic
p
-Laplacian systems with Lipschitz continuous coefficients.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
\mu\in(0,1]
and
p>2
.
There exists a constant
C
depending on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},C_{2}
such that for any weak solution
u
to the parabolic system (
3.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
with assumption (
3.2
), any
\sigma\in(0,1)
, any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, and any cylinder
Q_{R,S}\equiv Q_{R,S}(z_{o})\Subset E_{T}
with
R\in(0,1]
and
S>0
we have the quantitative
L^{\infty}
-gradient bound

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}}|Du|\leq\max\Bigg\{\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{p(N+2)}{4}}}\bigg[\frac{S/R^{2}}{(1-\sigma)^{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-2}},\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\}.

If we take
S\equiv\lambda^{2-p}R^{2}
we have the following straightforward consequence.

Corollary 3.12
.

In cylinders of the form

Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})=B_{R}(x_{o})\times\big(t_{o}-\lambda^{2-p}R^{2},t_{o}\big]

with
R\in(0,1]
, the gradient bound reads as

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{p(N+2)}{4}}}\bigg[\frac{\lambda^{2-p}}{(1-\sigma)^{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}},\varepsilon\lambda,\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\}.

Proof of Proposition
3.11
.

Writing the energy inequality from Proposition
3.1
with
z_{o}=(0,0)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in(-S,0]}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}\times\{\tau\}}v\zeta^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\iint_{Q_{R,S}}\underbrace{\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|D^{2}u|^{2}}_{\geq|Du|^{p-2}|D^{2}u|^{2}}\Phi(|Du|^{2})\zeta^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C\iint_{Q_{R,S}}\underbrace{\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|Du|^{2}}_{\leq C(p)(|Du|^{p}+\mu^{p})}\Phi(|Du|^{2})\big(\zeta^{2}+|\nabla\zeta|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\phantom{\leq\,}+C\iint_{Q_{R,S}}\underbrace{\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|Du|^{2}}_{\leq C(p)(|Du|^{p}+\mu^{p})}\Phi^{\prime}(|Du|^{2})|Du|^{2}\zeta^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\phantom{\leq\,}+C\iint_{Q_{R,S}}v\,\big|\partial_{t}\zeta^{2}\big|\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

The main difference to the case of constant or only time-dependent coefficients is the integral on the right-hand side containing the derivative
\Phi^{\prime}
. This term prevents us from choosing
\Phi(\tau):=\big(\sqrt{\tau}-k\big)_{+}^{p-2}
as in the case of constant coefficients, cf.
[
17
, p. 234, Lemma 4.2]
.
For this reason, we choose

\Phi(\tau):=\big(\sqrt{\tau}-k\big)_{+}^{p}.

The fact that
\Phi
is neither bounded nor Lipschitz is immaterial, since we have shown
|Du|
is locally bounded.
For
|Du|\geq k
, otherwise there is nothing to compute since in this case
v=0
, we have

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\int_{k^{2}}^{|Du|^{2}}\big(\sqrt{\tau}-k\big)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=2\int_{k}^{|Du|}(s-k)^{p}s\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{k}^{|Du|}(s-k)^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}s=\frac{2}{p+2}\big(|Du|-k\big)^{p+2}.

Similarly, we obtain the bound from above. In fact, we have

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=2\int_{k}^{|Du|}(s-k)^{p}s\,\mathrm{d}s\leq 2\int_{k}^{|Du|}s^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{2}{p+2}|Du|^{p+2}.

Hence,

\frac{2}{p+2}\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p+2}\leq v\leq\frac{2}{p+2}|Du|^{p+2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k\}}.

Next, we estimate the second term on the left-hand side from below.
Namely,

\displaystyle|Du|^{p-2}|D^{2}u|^{2}\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k\}}\underbrace{|Du|^{p-2}}_{\geq k^{p-2}}|D^{2}u|^{2}\big(|Du|-k\big)^{p}

\displaystyle\geq k^{p-2}\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p}|D^{2}u|^{2}.

Due to Kato’s inequality, the right-hand side of the last display can be further estimated from below if we observe that

\displaystyle\Big|\nabla\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{4}(p+2)^{2}\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p}\big|\nabla|Du|\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{4}(p+2)^{2}\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p}|D^{2}u|^{2}.

The last two estimates combined result in

\displaystyle|Du|^{p-2}

\displaystyle|D^{2}u|^{2}\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p}\geq\frac{4k^{p-2}}{(p+2)^{2}}\Big|\nabla\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\Big|^{2}.

It remains to consider the first and second terms on the right-hand side of the energy estimate. The first one can be estimated by

\displaystyle\big(|Du|^{p}+\mu^{p}\big)\Phi\big(|Du|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(|Du|^{p}+\mu^{p}\big)\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k\}}\big(|Du|^{2p}+\mu^{p}|Du|^{p}\big),

while the second one can be bounded by

\displaystyle\big(|Du|^{p}+\mu^{p}\big)\Phi^{\prime}\big(|Du|^{2}\big)|Du|^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}p\big(|Du|^{p}+\mu^{p}\big)\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p-1}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k\}}\big(|Du|^{2p}+\mu^{p}|Du|^{p}\big).

Using the estimates of the individual terms in the energy inequality, we eventually obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in(-S,0]}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}\times\{\tau\}}\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{p+2}\zeta^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+k^{p-2}\iint_{Q_{R,S}}\Big|\nabla\big(|Du|-k\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\Big|^{2}\zeta^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{Q_{R,S}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k\}}\big(|Du|^{2p}+\mu^{p}|Du|^{p}\big)\big(|\nabla\zeta|^{2}+\zeta^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p+2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k\}}\big|\partial_{t}\zeta^{2}\big|\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

with a constant
C=C(p,C_{o},C_{1},C_{2})
.
Note that the preceding energy inequality holds true for any general cylinder
Q_{R,S}\subset E_{T}
, for any
k>0
, and any cut-off function
\zeta
vanishing on the parabolic boundary
\partial_{\mathrm{par}}Q_{R,S}
. Now, we define shrinking families of cylinders
Q_{n}:=Q_{\varrho_{n},\theta_{n}}
and
\widetilde{Q}_{n}:=Q_{\tilde{\varrho}_{n},\tilde{\theta}_{n}}
, where

\varrho_{n}
,
\tilde{\varrho}_{n}
,
\theta_{n}
,
\tilde{\theta}_{n}
are defined according to

\varrho_{n}:=\sigma R+\frac{1-\sigma}{2^{n}}R,\quad\ \theta_{n}:=\sigma S+\frac{1-\sigma}{2^{n}}S

and

\tilde{\varrho}_{n}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\varrho_{n}+\varrho_{n+1}\big)=\sigma R+\frac{3(1-\sigma)}{2^{n+2}}R,\quad\tilde{\theta}_{n}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\theta_{n}+\theta_{n+1}\big)=\sigma S+\frac{3(1-\sigma)}{2^{n+2}}S.

These cylinders are arranged in such a way that
Q_{n+1}\subset\widetilde{Q}_{n}\subset Q_{n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. The level
k
in the energy estimate is fixed by an increasing sequence of levels

k_{n}:=k-\frac{k}{2^{n}},

where
k>0
is a number to be determined in a universal way later in the course of the proof. Since the above energy inequality shall be applied on the cylinders
Q_{n}
, we specify the cutoff function

\zeta\equiv\zeta_{n}
in such a way that it vanishes on the parabolic boundary of
Q_{n}
on the one hand, and is identical to 1 on the intermediate cylinder
\widetilde{Q}_{n}
on the other hand. Moreover, we require that

|\nabla\zeta_{n}|\leq\frac{2^{n+3}}{(1-\sigma)R},\quad\mbox{and}\quad|\partial_{t}\zeta_{n}|\leq\frac{2^{n+3}}{(1-\sigma)S}.

With respect to these choices, the energy inequality for
Q_{n}
reads as follows

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in(-\theta_{n},0]}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{n}\times\{\tau\}}\Big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\Big]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+\underbrace{k_{n+1}^{p-2}}_{\geq(k/2)^{p-2}}\iint_{Q_{n}}\Big|\nabla\big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\big]\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C2^{2n}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}}\iint_{Q_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\big(|Du|^{2p}+\mu^{p}|Du|^{p}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(3.25)

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+\frac{C2^{n}}{(1-\sigma)S}\iint_{Q_{{}_{n}}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}|Du|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

To shorten the notation we introduce

\bm{Y}_{n}:=\iint_{Q_{n}}\big(|Du|-k_{n}\big)_{+}^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

The integral
\bm{Y}_{n}
can be easily estimated from below by reducing the domain of integration. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{n}

\displaystyle\geq\iint_{Q_{n}\cap\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\big(|Du|-k_{n}\big)_{+}^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq\big(k_{n+1}-k_{n}\big)^{p+2}\Big|Q_{n}\cap\big\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\big\}\Big|

\displaystyle=\frac{k^{p+2}}{2^{(n+1){(p+2)}}}\Big|Q_{n}\cap\big\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\big\}\Big|.

Next, we estimate
\bm{Y}_{n+1}
from above. For this purpose we first increase in
\bm{Y}_{n+1}
the domain of integration from
Q_{n+1}
to
\widetilde{Q}_{n}
and then apply Hölder’s inequality with exponents
\frac{N+2}{N}
and
\frac{N+2}{2}
. Subsequently, we use the fact that
\zeta_{n}=1
on
\widetilde{Q}_{n}
and the last displayed measure estimate. In this way we obtain

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}}\Big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\Big]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{n}}\Big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\Big]^{2\frac{N+2}{N}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{N}{N+2}}\Big|\widetilde{Q}_{n}\cap\big\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\big\}\Big|^{\frac{2}{N+2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\iint_{Q_{n}}\Big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\Big]^{2\frac{N+2}{N}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{N}{N+2}}\bigg[\frac{2^{(n+1)(p+2)}}{k^{p+2}}\bm{Y}_{n}\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N+2}}.

The first integral on the right-hand side can be controlled using Sobolev’s inequality
[
17
, Chapter I, Proposition 3.1]
with
p=2
and
m=2
. The application yields

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{n}}

\displaystyle\Big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\Big]^{2\frac{N+2}{N}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{Q_{n}}\Big|\nabla\big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\big]\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,C}\cdot\bigg[\sup_{\tau\in(-\theta_{n},0]}\int_{B_{n}\times\{\tau\}}\Big[\big(|Du|-k_{n+1}\big)_{+}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\zeta_{n}\Big]^{2}\mathrm{d}x\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N}}

\displaystyle\leq Ck^{-(p-2)}\big[\mbox{right-hand side of \eqref{est:en-DG-p>2}}\big]^{\frac{N+2}{N}}.

To obtain the last line, we used (
3.6
). Inserting this inequality in the estimate of
\bm{Y}_{n+1}
we obtain

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C2^{n(p+4)}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}}k^{-\frac{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}{N+2}}\bm{Y}_{n}^{\frac{2}{N+2}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\bigg[\frac{1}{R^{2}}\iint_{Q_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\big(|Du|^{2p}+\mu^{p}|Du|^{p}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg[\,}+\frac{1}{S}\iint_{Q_{{}_{n}}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}|Du|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg].

To derive a homogeneous estimate we reduce the power of
|Du|
in the first integral from
2p
to
p+2
and estimate a part of

|Du|^{2p}
by its supremum. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\big(|Du|^{2p}+\mu^{p}|Du|^{p}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\frac{\mu^{p}}{k^{p}}\Big)\iint_{Q_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}|Du|^{2p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\sup_{Q_{n}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2}\Big(1+\frac{\mu^{p}}{k^{p}}\Big)\iint_{Q_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}|Du|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

To obtain the second-to-last line we used that
|Du|>k_{n+1}\geq\frac{1}{2}k
on the domain of integration.
Furthermore, reducing the domain of integration from
Q_{n}
to the superlevel set
Q_{n}\cap\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}
and using the relation between
k_{n}
and
k_{n+1}
, i.e.

k_{n}=k_{n+1}\frac{2^{n+1}-2}{2^{n+1}-1},

we obtain

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{n}

\displaystyle\geq\iint_{Q_{n}}\big(|Du|-k_{n}\big)_{+}^{p+2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\iint_{Q_{n}}\bigg[|Du|-k_{n+1}\frac{2^{n+1}-2}{2^{n+1}-1}\bigg]^{p+2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq\iint_{Q_{n}}|Du|^{p+2}\bigg[1-\frac{2^{n+1}-2}{2^{n+1}-1}\bigg]^{p+2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2^{(n+1)(p+2)}}\iint_{Q_{n}}|Du|^{p+2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

so that

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}|Du|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq 2^{(n+1)(p+2)}\bm{Y}_{n}.

Inserting this inequality above we obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|Du|>k_{n+1}\}}

\displaystyle\big(|Du|^{2p}+\mu^{p}|Du|^{p}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\sup_{Q_{n}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2}\bigg(1+\frac{\mu^{p}}{k^{p}}\bigg)2^{(n+1)(p+2)}\bm{Y}_{n}.

However, this leads us to the recursive inequality

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C2^{2n(p+3)}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}}k^{-\frac{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}{N+2}}\bm{Y}_{n}^{1+\frac{2}{N+2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{R^{2}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{n}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2}\bigg(1+\frac{\mu^{p}}{k^{p}}\bigg)+\frac{1}{S}\bigg].

Here, we require that
k\geq\varepsilon\mu
. Now, if for some
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

\displaystyle\bigg(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\sup_{Q_{n}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R^{2}}{S},

the claimed
L^{\infty}
-gradient bound follows since
Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}\subset Q_{n}
which will yield

(3.26)

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}}|Du|\leq\sup_{Q_{n}}|Du|\leq\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}.

Otherwise, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the recursive inequality simplifies to

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\bm{b}^{n}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}k^{-\frac{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}{N+2}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{n}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2}\bm{Y}_{n}^{1+\frac{2}{N+2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\bm{b}^{n}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}k^{-\frac{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}{N+2}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2}\bm{Y}_{n}^{1+\frac{2}{N+2}}

with
\bm{b}:=4^{p+3}
.
At this stage, we
apply the lemma of geometric convergence
[
17
, Chapter I, Lemma 4.1]
with
C,\alpha
replaced by

\frac{C\bm{}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}k^{-\frac{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}{N+2}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tfrac{2}{N+2},

to conclude that
\bm{Y}_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
provided that

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{o}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{C}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}k^{-\frac{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}{N+2}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|\bigg)^{p-2}\bigg]^{-\frac{N+2}{2}}\bm{b}^{-\frac{(N+2)^{2}}{4}}.

We resolve this inequality for
k
, and obtain that
k
must satisfy

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\geq\bigg[\frac{C\bm{b}^{\frac{N+2}{2}}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|\bigg)^{\frac{(N+2)(p-2)}{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg[\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p+2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}}.

Apparently, the integrability exponent in the integral on the right-hand side can be reduced by estimating a part of the integrand by the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
|Du|
. This leads to a stronger condition on
k
. The use of

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p+2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sup_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|\bigg)^{2}\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

leads to the requirement

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\geq\bigg[\frac{C\bm{b}^{\frac{N+2}{2}}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|\bigg)^{\frac{N(p-2)+2p}{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg[\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{N(p-2)+2(p+2)}}.

Let us name the right-hand side as
\bm{K}
and
choose
k
as
\max\{\bm{K},\varepsilon\mu\}
. Then,
from
\bm{Y}_{\infty}=0
we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\max\{\bm{K},\varepsilon\mu\}.

In combination with (
3.26
), which holds in the other case, this results in

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\max\bigg\{\bm{K},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-2}},\varepsilon\mu\bigg\}.

To set up an interpolation argument we first re-write the last display in a more compact form using the abbreviations

\bm{Y}_{o}=\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,\quad\mbox{and}\quad\bm{\nu}:=N(p-2)+2p.

Namely, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}}|Du|\leq\max\Bigg\{\bigg[\frac{C}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bigg(\sup_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|\bigg)^{\frac{\bm{\nu}}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{\frac{2}{\bm{\nu}+4}},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-2}},\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\}.

Next, we define a sequence of nested cylinders
\widehat{Q}_{n}=\widehat{Q}_{\varrho_{n},\theta_{n}}
with
\varrho_{o}=\sigma R
, with
\theta_{o}=\sigma S
, and
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
with

\varrho_{n}:=\sigma R+(1-\sigma)R\sum_{j=1}^{n}2^{-j}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\theta_{n}:=\sigma S+(1-\sigma)S\sum_{j=1}^{n}2^{-j}.

Observe that
\widehat{Q}_{o}=Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}
and
\widehat{Q}_{\infty}=Q_{R,S}
. Moreover, we define

\bm{M}_{n}:=\sup_{\widehat{Q}_{n}}|Du|

and apply the above gradient bound on two consecutive cylinders
\widehat{Q}_{n+1}\supset\widehat{Q}_{n}
instead of

Q_{R,S}\supset Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}
.
Moreover, we use the facts
(\varrho_{n+1}-\varrho_{n})^{2}=(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}2^{-2(n+1)}

and

\frac{\varrho_{n+1}^{2}}{\theta_{n+1}}=\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big[\sigma+(1-\sigma)\sum_{j=1}^{n}2^{-j}\Big]\leq\frac{R^{2}}{S}.

This results in recursive inequalities of the type

\displaystyle\bm{M}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{\bigg[\frac{C}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}2^{\frac{2(n+1)(N+2)}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bm{M}_{n+1}^{\frac{\bm{\nu}}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{\frac{2}{\bm{\nu}+4}},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-2}},\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\}.

If for some
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
either the second or the third entry in the maximum dominates the first one, there is nothing to prove, since we trivially have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}}|Du|=\bm{M}_{o}\leq\bm{M}_{n}\leq\max\bigg\{\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-2}},\varepsilon\mu\bigg\}.

Otherwise, the first term in the maximum dominates for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the second and third entry, so that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\bm{M}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{C}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{\frac{2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\big(\underbrace{4^{\frac{N+2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}}_{=:\bm{b}}\big)^{n}\bm{M}_{n+1}^{1-\frac{4}{\bm{\nu}+4}}.

To the preceding recursive inequalities we apply the Interpolation Lemma
[
17
, Chapter I, Lemma 4.3]
with
\alpha
,
C
replaced by

\frac{4}{\bm{\nu}+4},\qquad\bigg[\frac{C\varepsilon^{-p}}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}}\bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{\frac{2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}.

The application yields

\displaystyle\bm{M}_{o}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[2\bigg[\frac{C}{(1-\sigma)^{2}R^{2}\varepsilon^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bm{Y}_{o}^{\frac{2}{\bm{\nu}+4}}\bm{b}^{\frac{\bm{\nu}}{4}}\Bigg]^{\frac{\bm{\nu}+4}{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{p(N+2)}{4}}}\frac{1}{[(1-\sigma)R]^{\frac{N+2}{2}}}\bigg[\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{p(N+2)}{4}}}\frac{\sqrt{S/R^{2}}}{(1-\sigma)^{\frac{N+2}{2}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Taking into account the bound from the trivial case, we conclude that claimed gradient bound.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

With an interpolation argument as in the previous proof, the integrability exponent
p
can be further reduced. In fact, for any
q\in(p-2,p)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma S}(z_{o})}|Du|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max\Bigg\{\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{p(N+2)}{2(q+2-p)}}}\bigg[\frac{S/R^{2}}{(1-\sigma)^{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}|Du|^{q}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q+2-p}},\varepsilon\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{S}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-2}},\varepsilon\mu\Bigg\}.

Remark 3.14
.

Exactly as already pointed out in Remark
3.7
,
when
p=2
, we can take
\varepsilon=1
,
\mu=0
,
S=R^{2}
. Hence, the previous estimate reduces to

\sup_{Q_{\sigma R,\sigma R^{2}}(z_{o})}|Du|\leq\Bigg[\frac{C}{(1-\sigma)^{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R,R^{2}}(z_{o})}|Du|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}},

for any
q\in(0,2]
.

4.
p
-Laplace systems with measurable coefficients

This section mainly provides some comparison estimates that are necessary to prove the Schauder-type estimates. In contrast to the previous section, we consider systems of
p
-Laplace-type with coefficients that are merely measurable, and we include the case
\mu=0
. More precisely, we study weak solutions to the parabolic system (
1.1
) under the assumptions (
1.2
)
1
. The assumption (
1.2
)
2
, i.e. the Hölder continuity with respect to the spatial variable, is only used in Lemma
4.7
, when we show a comparison estimate in preparation for the proof of Schauder estimates.

4.1.
Zero order energy estimates

Weak solutions to the parabolic system (
1.1
) satisfy a basic energy estimate, which follows in a straightforward way by testing the system against
(u-\xi)\zeta^{p}
,
with
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and a suitable cut-off function
\zeta
, and modulo a Steklov averaging procedure.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that
p>1
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, and that the coefficient
a
satisfies
(
1.2
)
1
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(p,C_{o},C_{1})
such that
for any weak solution
u
to the parabolic system (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
, for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and any pair of cylinders
Q_{r,s}(z_{o})\subset Q_{R,S}(z_{o})\subset E_{T}
with
0<s<S
,

0<r<R
, we have the energy estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in(t_{o}-s,t_{o}]}\int_{B_{r}(x_{o})\times\{\tau\}}

\displaystyle|u-\xi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\iint_{Q_{r,s}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}\bigg[\frac{|u-\xi|^{p}}{(R-r)^{p}}+\frac{|u-\xi|^{2}}{S-s}+\mu^{p}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Remark 4.2
.

For cylinders
Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset E_{T}
with
\tfrac{1}{2}R\leq r<R
, after passing to mean values the energy inequality of Proposition
4.1

takes the form

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{p-2}}{R^{2}}\sup_{\tau\in(t_{o}-\lambda^{2-p}r^{2},t_{o}]}

\displaystyle-\mskip-19.5mu\int_{B_{r}(x_{o})\times\{\tau\}}|u-\xi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\bigg[\frac{|u-\xi|^{p}}{(R-r)^{p}}+\lambda^{p-2}\frac{|u-\xi|^{2}}{(R-r)^{2}}+\mu^{p}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where now the constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1})
additionally depends on the dimension
N
.

4.2.
Comparison principle

Needless to say, a comparison principle does not hold for systems in general. However, it does hold when the system has certain special structures.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
p>1
and
\mu\in[0,1]
. Let
u

be a weak solution to

\partial_{t}v-\Div\Big(a(x_{o},t)\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv\Big)=0\quad\mbox{in $Q_{R,S}(z_{o})$}

in the sense of Definition
2.6
, and
\xi,\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Suppose that
v^{i}\leq\xi^{i}
on
\partial_{\mathrm{par}}Q_{R,S}(z_{o})

for any
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
. Then
v^{i}\leq\xi^{i}
a.e. in
Q_{R,S}(z_{o})
. Moreover,
v^{i}\geq\zeta^{i}
on
\partial_{\mathrm{par}}Q_{R,S}(z_{o})

for any
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
implies
v^{i}\geq\zeta^{i}
a.e. in
Q_{R,S}(z_{o})
.

On the lateral boundary
\partial B_{R}(x_{o})\times(t_{o}-S,t_{o})
the condition
v^{i}\leq\xi^{i}
has to be understood in the sense that
(v^{i}-\xi^{i})_{+}\in L^{p}\big(t_{o}-S,t_{o};W^{1,p}_{0}(B_{R}(x_{o}))\big)
, while at the initial time
t_{o}-S
it is to be understood in terms of the pointwise condition
v^{i}(\cdot,t_{o}-S)\leq\xi^{i}
a.e. on
B_{R}(x_{o})
.

Proof..

We only consider the case where
v^{i}\leq\xi^{i}
on
\partial_{\mathrm{par}}Q_{R,S}(z_{o})
. The other one is similar.
Fix a time
\tau\in(t_{o}-S,t_{o})
and choose in the weak formulation of the parabolic system for
v
the testing function

\varphi(x,t)=\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau\}}(v(x,t)-\xi)_{+},

where

(v-\xi)_{+}=\big((v^{1}-\xi^{1})_{+},\dots,(v^{k}-\xi^{k})_{+}\big).

This leads to

\iint_{B_{R}(x_{o})\times(t_{o}-S,\tau)}\partial_{t}(v-\xi)\cdot(v-\xi)_{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t=-\mathbf{II},

where the right-hand side is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}

\displaystyle:=\iint_{B_{R}(x_{o})\times(t_{o}-S,\tau)}a(x_{o},t)\mathsf{A}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

with
\mathsf{A}
defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle:=\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv\cdot D(v-\xi)_{+}.

Observe that
\mathsf{A}\geq 0
,
and therefore the integral
\mathbf{II}\geq 0
is non-negative. This allows us to conclude that

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{B_{R}(x_{o})\times\{\tau\}}

\displaystyle|(v-\xi)_{+}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x-\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{B_{R}(x_{o})\times\{t_{o}-S\}}|(v-\xi)_{+}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{=0}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{B_{R}(x_{o})\times(t_{o}-S,\tau)}\partial_{t}|(v-\xi)_{+}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\iint_{B_{R}(x_{o})\times(t_{o}-\tau,\tau)}\partial_{t}(v-\xi)\cdot(v-\xi)_{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{II}\leq 0.

Here we used that
(v-\xi)_{+}(\cdot,t_{o}-S)=0
in
B_{R}(x_{o})
.
Hence,
v^{i}(\cdot,\tau)\leq\xi^{i}
a.e. in
B_{R}(x_{o})
for any
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
. Since
\tau\in(t_{o}-S,t_{o})
was arbitrary, this shows
v^{i}\leq\xi^{i}
a.e. in
Q_{R,S}(z_{o})
for any
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
. This proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
p>1
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, and
u
a bounded weak solution to the parabolic
p
-Laplace-type system (
1.1
) in
E_{T}
. Let
Q_{R,S}(z_{o})\subset E_{T}
and
v

be the unique weak solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\partial_{t}v-\Div\Big(a(x_{o},t)\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv\Big)=0,&\mbox{in $Q_{R,S}(z_{o})$}\\[7.0pt]
v=u,&\mbox{on $\partial_{\mathrm{par}}Q_{R,S}(z_{o})$}\end{array}\right.

in the sense of Definition
2.5
.
Then,

\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}v\leq\sqrt{k}\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}u,

where the oscillation has to be understood with respect to the euclidean norm.

Proof..

Abbreviate
Q:=Q_{R,S}(z_{o})
and
apply the previous comparison Lemma with
\xi^{i}:=\sup_{Q}u^{i}
respectively
\zeta^{i}:=\inf_{Q}u^{i}
. We
deduce

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}v^{i}\leq\sup_{Q}u^{i}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\inf_{Q}v^{i}\geq\inf_{Q}u^{i}\qquad\mbox{for }i=1,\ldots,k,

so that for a.e.
(x,t),(\tilde{x},\tilde{t})\in Q
we have

\displaystyle-\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q}u^{i}=\inf_{Q}u^{i}-\sup_{Q}u^{i}\leq v^{i}(x,t)-v^{i}(\tilde{x},\tilde{t})\leq\sup_{Q}u^{i}-\inf_{Q}u^{i}=\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q}u^{i}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle|v(x,t)-v(\tilde{x},\tilde{t})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v^{i}(x,t)-v^{i}(\tilde{x},\tilde{t})|^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\big(\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q}u^{i}\big)^{2}\leq k\big(\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q}u\big)^{2}

for a.e.
(x,t),(\tilde{x},\tilde{t})\in Q
. This implies the claim.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

The previous result has been proved for cylinders whose cross-sections are balls, but any kind of set besides balls can do here.

4.3.
Comparison estimates

Let
p>1
,
\mu\in(0,1]
and let

v\in L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap C\big([0,T];L^{2}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)

be a weak solution to the parabolic
p
-Laplace-type system (
1.1
),
where the coefficients
a(x,t)
satisfy the assumptions (
1.2
)
1
.
Let

w\in L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap C\big([0,T];L^{2}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)

solve

(4.1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\partial_{t}w-\Div\Big(b(x,t)\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dw\Big)=0,&\mbox{in $E_{T}$,}\\[6.0pt]
w=v,&\mbox{on $\partial_{\mathrm{par}}E_{T}$}.\end{array}\right.

The coefficients
b\in L^{\infty}(E_{T})
are assumed to satisfy

(4.2)

b(x,t)\geq C_{o}>0

for a.e.
(x,t)\in E_{T}
. The following comparison estimate is the main result of this section.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
v
be a weak solution to the parabolic system (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
and
w
be a weak solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem (
4.1
) with (
4.2
) in the sense of Definition
2.5
.
Then, we have the comparison estimate

(4.3)

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\big[\mathsf{A}^{p}+\mathsf{A}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\big]\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where we used the abbreviation
\mathsf{A}:=\|a-b\|_{L^{\infty}(E_{T})}
. In the super-quadratic case

p\geq 2
we have the stronger comparison estimate

(4.4)

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\mathsf{A}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Furthermore, for any
p>1
we have the energy inequality

(4.5)

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\big[1+\mathsf{A}^{p}+\mathsf{A}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\big]\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

In all estimates, the constant
C
depends only on
p
and
C_{o}
.

Proof..

Writing down the Steklov formulation of the parabolic systems for
v
and
w
on
Q

and subtracting from one another, we obtain

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle-\iint_{E_{T}}[v-w]_{h}\cdot\partial_{t}\zeta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\iint_{E_{T}}\Big[a\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv-b\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dw\Big]_{h}\cdot D\zeta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

for any

\zeta\in W^{1,2}\big(0,T;L^{2}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)

such that
\zeta(\cdot,0)=0=\zeta(\cdot,T)
. We now choose
\zeta=[v-w]_{h}\psi_{\varepsilon}
. Here the Lipschitz function
\psi_{\varepsilon}\colon[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}

is chosen to be equal to
1
in
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset(0,T)
, vanishes outside

[t_{1}-\varepsilon,t_{2}+\varepsilon]
with
0<\varepsilon<\min\big\{t_{1},T-t_{2}\big\}
, and is otherwise linearly interpolated. In this context,
[v-w]_{h}
is the Steklov average of
v-w
and
0<h<T-(t_{2}+\varepsilon)
. In the above Steklov formulation
there are two terms to be treated. We start with the term containing the time derivative. This term is calculated as follows

\displaystyle-\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle[v-w]_{h}\cdot\partial_{t}\big([v-w]_{h}\psi_{\varepsilon}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{E_{T}}\partial_{t}\big|[v-w]_{h}\big|^{2}\psi_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t-\iint_{E_{T}}\big|[v-w]_{h}\big|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{E_{T}}\big|[v-w]_{h}\big|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Using Lemma
2.4
(i) we obtain

-\lim_{h\downarrow 0}\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{E_{T}}\big|[v-w]_{h}\big|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t=-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{E_{T}}|v-w|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Here we pass to the limit
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
and obtain

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\lim_{h\downarrow 0}

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{E_{T}}\big|[v-w]_{h}\big|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{E\times\{t_{2}\}}|v-w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x-\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{E\times\{t_{1}\}}|v-w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

The first integral yields a non-negative contribution and can be discarded, while the second integral converges to
0
at
t_{1}\downarrow 0
due to the initial condition in the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem (
4.1
). The remaining term in the above Steklov
formulation converges to

\iint_{E\times(t_{1},t_{2})}\Big[a\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv-b\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dw\Big]\cdot D(v-w)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

if we first let
h\downarrow 0
and then
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
. The limit
h\downarrow 0
can be justified with Lemma
2.4
(ii). Consequently, after letting
t_{1}\downarrow 0
and
t_{2}\uparrow T
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle b\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv-\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dw\Big]\cdot(Dv-Dw)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{E_{T}}(b-a)\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv\cdot(Dv-Dw)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Using the monotonicity of the vector-field
\xi\mapsto\big(\mu^{2}+|\xi|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\xi
stated in Lemma
2.3
, and the bound from below for the coefficients
b
from (
4.2
) (which is
C_{o}
), the
left-hand side can be bounded from below, while the right-hand side is estimated from above using
Hölder’s inequality. This way we get

\displaystyle\tfrac{C_{o}}{C(p)}\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle:=\tfrac{C_{o}}{C(p)}\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|Dv-Dw|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.6)

\displaystyle\,\leq\mathsf{A}\bigg[\underbrace{\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}_{:=\mathsf{E}}\bigg]^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}.

In the case
p\geq 2
this immediately implies (
4.4
). Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv

\displaystyle-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq 2^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mathbf{I}\leq\tfrac{C(p)}{C_{o}}\mathsf{A}\mathsf{E}^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\bigg[\iint_{Q}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}},

which yields

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\mathsf{A}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\mathsf{E}

for a constant
C=C(p,C_{o})
. In the case
1<p<2
, however, we apply first Hölder’s inequality with exponents
\frac{2}{p}
and
\frac{2}{2-p}
and then (
4.3
) to obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{I}^{\frac{p}{2}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}+|Dw-Dv+Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2-p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathsf{A}^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathsf{E}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\mathsf{E}\bigg]^{\frac{2-p}{2}},

which is the same as

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv

\displaystyle-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\mathsf{A}^{p}\mathsf{E}^{p-1}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\mathsf{E}\bigg]^{2-p}.

Applying Young’s inequality with exponents
\frac{1}{p-1}
and
\frac{1}{2-p}
we get

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\big[\mathsf{A}^{p}+\mathsf{A}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\big]\mathsf{E}.

After re-absorbing the integral of
|Dv-Dw|^{p}
to the left-hand side, we
obtain the asserted comparison estimate (
4.3
)
also in the sub-quadratic case. The
energy estimate (
4.5
) follows from

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C(p)\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p,C_{o})\big[1+\mathsf{A}^{p}+\mathsf{A}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\big]\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

This completes
the proof of the lemma.
∎

Here, we record an application of the last lemma; it will be useful when dealing with the
p
-Laplace system (
1.1
) that has Hölder continuous coefficients as in (
1.2
). The comparison function
w
is then taken as the unique weak solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem
on some cylinder
Q_{R,S}(z_{o})
with frozen coefficients
a(x_{o},t)
.
That is, we denote by

w\in L^{p}\big(t_{o}-S,t_{o};W^{1,p}(B_{R}(x_{o}),\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap C\big([t_{o}-S,t_{o}];L^{2}(B_{R}(x_{o}),\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)

the unique weak solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem

(4.7)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\partial_{t}w-\Div\Big(a(x_{o},t)\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dw\Big)=0,&\mbox{in $Q_{R,S}(z_{o})$,}\\[6.0pt]
w=u,&\mbox{on $\partial_{\mathrm{par}}Q_{R,S}(z_{o})$.}\end{array}\right.

In this situation we have the following result.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to the parabolic system (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
with assumption (
1.2
)
and
w
be the unique weak solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem (
4.7
) on some cylinder
Q_{R,S}(z_{o})\subset E_{T}
with
R\leq 1
in the sense of Definition
2.5
.
Then, we have the comparison estimate

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle|Du-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq CR^{\alpha_{\ast}p}\iint_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Here, the exponent
\alpha_{\ast}
is given by

(4.8)

\alpha_{\ast}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\alpha,&\mbox{if $1<p<2$,}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\alpha}{p-1},&\mbox{if $p\geq 2$.}\end{array}\right.

Furthermore, we have the energy inequality

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\iint_{Q_{R,S}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

In all estimates, the constant
C
depends only on
p
,
C_{o}
and
C_{1}
.

Proof..

We apply Lemma
4.6
with
b(x,t):=a(x_{o},t)
.
Here, we only need to determine the pre-factor in the estimates in this specific situation. Since

\displaystyle\|a-b\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{R,S}(z_{o})})}\leq C_{1}R^{\alpha},

we have

\displaystyle\|a-b\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{R,S}(z_{o})})}^{p}+\|a-b\|_{L^{\infty}({Q_{R,S}(z_{o})})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\leq C_{1}^{p}R^{\alpha p}+C_{1}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}R^{\frac{\alpha p}{p-1}}\leq CR^{\alpha_{\ast}p}\leq C.

The claimed inequalities immediately follow.
∎

At a later stage, we will need a version of the comparison estimate when
v
is a solution to the degenerate parabolic
p
-Laplace system (
1.1
) with
\mu=0
and the comparison mapping is a solution to the non-degenerate
p
-Laplace system (
4.1
) with
\mu\in(0,1]
.
More precisely let

v\in L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap C\big([0,T];L^{2}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)

be a weak solution to the parabolic
p
-Laplace-type system

(4.9)

\partial_{t}v-\Div\big(a(x,t)|Dv|^{p-2}Dv\big)=0\quad\mbox{in $E_{T}$,}

where the coefficient
a(x,t)
satisfies the assumption (
1.2
)
1
, and let

w\in L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap C\big([0,T];L^{2}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)

be the unique solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem (
4.1
) in
E_{T}
with
\mu\in(0,1]
and with coefficients
b
satisfying (
4.2
).

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
v
be a weak solution to the parabolic system (
4.9
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
with coefficient
a
satisfying (
1.2
)
1
,
and let
w
be the unique weak solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem (
4.1
) with (
4.2
) in the sense of Definition
2.5
.
Then, we have the comparison estimate

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\big[(\mathsf{A}+\mu)^{p}+(\mathsf{A}+\mu)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\big]\iint_{E_{T}}\big(1+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where we used the abbreviation
\mathsf{A}:=\|a-b\|_{L^{\infty}(E_{T})}
. In the super-quadratic case

p\geq 2
the inequality holds without the term
(\mathsf{A}+\mu)^{p}
, i.e. we have

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\mathsf{A}+\mu)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\iint_{E_{T}}\big(1+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Furthermore, for any
p>1
we have the energy inequality

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[1+(\mathsf{A}+\mu)^{p}+(\mathsf{A}+\mu)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\Big]\iint_{E_{T}}\big(1+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

In all estimates, the constant
C
depends on
p,C_{o}
and
C_{1}
.

Proof..

The reasoning proceeds largely along the lines of the proof of the comparison Lemma
4.6
. In principle, we only have to replace
\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv
by
|Dv|^{p-2}Dv
in the argument. This leads to

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle b\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv-\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dw\Big]\cdot(Dv-Dw)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{E_{T}}\Big[b\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv-a|Dv|^{p-2}Dv\Big]\cdot(Dv-Dw)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\iint_{E_{T}}(b-a)\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv\cdot(Dv-Dw)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\mathbf{II},

where we abbreviated

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}

\displaystyle:=\iint_{E_{T}}a\Big[\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}-|Dv|^{p-2}\Big]Dv\cdot(Dv-Dw)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Taking into account that

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}-|Dv|^{p-2}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\mu\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-3}{2}},

and using Hölder’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathbf{II}|

\displaystyle\leq C(p)C_{1}\mu\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-3}{2}}|Dv||Dv-Dw|\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)C_{1}\mu\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|Dv-Dw|\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)C_{1}\mu\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}\frac{p}{p-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}.

In the case
p\geq 2
we have
0\leq\frac{p-2}{p-1}\leq 1
, so that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{II}|

\displaystyle\leq C(p)C_{1}\mu\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}\big(1+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}},

while in the case
1<p<2
we have
\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\leq\mu^{p-2}
, so that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{II}|

\displaystyle\leq C(p)C_{1}\mu^{p-1}|E_{T}|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)C_{1}\mu^{p-1}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}\big(1+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\bigg[\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Together with the estimates for the left-hand side and the first term on the right-hand side from the proof of Lemma
4.6
, we get in the super-quadratic case
p\geq 2

that

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv

\displaystyle-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\big(\mathsf{A}+\mu\big)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\iint_{E_{T}}\big(1+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

for a constant
C=C(p,C_{o},C_{1})
, while for exponents
1<p<2
, the arguments from the proof of Lemma
4.6
and the estimate for
\mathbf{II}
yield

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv

\displaystyle-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big[\big(\mathsf{A}+\mu\big)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+(\mathsf{A}+\mu)^{p}\Big]\iint_{E_{T}}\big(1+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

again with a constant
C=C(p,C_{o},C_{1})
.
The energy inequality is obtained by combining

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{T}}

\displaystyle\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\iint_{E_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C(p)\iint_{E_{T}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

with the comparison estimate.
∎

4.4.
Gluing Lemma and oscillation estimates for Lipschitz solutions

The first lemma of this section serves to compare slice-wise mean values at different times. Such a result is often called
Gluing Lemma
. In the formulation of the lemma,
\eta
stands for a non-negative function in
C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{1})
with
\|\eta\|_{L^{1}(B_{1})}=1
, whereas
\eta_{R}(x):=R^{-N}\eta\big(\frac{x}{R}\big)
is equal to
1
on
B_{R}
and retains a unit integral.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
p>1
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, and
u
be a weak solution to the parabolic system (
1.1
) with (
1.2
)
1
in the sense of Definition
2.6
. Assume that
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(E_{T})
. Then, for every cylinder
Q=B_{R}(x_{o})\times(\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]\Subset E_{T}
and any
\tau_{1}<t_{1}<t_{2}<\tau_{2}
we have

\bigg|\int_{B_{R}(x_{o})}\big[u(\cdot,t_{2})-u(\cdot,t_{1})\big]\eta_{R}(\cdot-x_{o})\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\leq C\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{R}\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}

for a constant
C=C(N,C_{1})
.

Proof..

In the proof we assume
x_{o}=0
.
We define
\xi_{\varepsilon}=\xi_{\varepsilon}(t)
to be
1
in
[t_{1},t_{2}]
,

0
outside
[t_{1}-\varepsilon,t_{2}+\varepsilon]
, and linearly interpolated otherwise; here, we assume
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\min\{t_{1}-\tau_{1},\tau_{2}-t_{2}\}\big)
.
Then we test the weak form of (
1.1
) for a fixed index
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
with

\varphi(x,t):=\xi_{\varepsilon}(t)\eta_{R}(x)\mathrm{e}_{i},

where
\mathrm{e}_{i}
denotes the
i
-th canonical basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. In the limit
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}

\displaystyle\big[u(\cdot,t_{2})-u(\cdot,t_{1})]\cdot\mathrm{e}_{i}\eta_{R}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\iint_{B_{R}\times(t_{1},t_{2})}a\,\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Du\cdot(\mathrm{e}_{i}\otimes\nabla\eta_{R})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

We multiply the preceding identity by
\mathrm{e}_{i}
and sum over
i=1,\dots,k
. This yields

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}

\displaystyle\big[u(\cdot,t_{2})-u(\cdot,t_{1})]\eta_{R}\,\mathrm{d}x=\iint_{B_{R}\times(t_{1},t_{2})}a\,\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Du\cdot\nabla\eta_{R}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Recalling the upper bound for the coefficients
a
from (
1.2
)
1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{B_{R}}\big[u(\cdot,t_{2})-u(\cdot,t_{1})]\eta_{R}\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(N)C_{1}}{R^{N+1}}\iint_{B_{R}\times(t_{1},t_{2})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{R}\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-1}{2}},

proving the asserted gluing estimate.
∎

Next, we collect two
a priori
oscillation estimates of weak solutions whose spatial gradient is assumed to be locally bounded.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
p>1
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, and
u
be a weak solution to the parabolic
p
-Laplace system (
1.1
) with (
1.2
)
1
in the sense of Definition
2.6
. Assume that
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(E_{T})
. Then, for every cylinder
Q=B_{R}(x_{o})\times(\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]\Subset E_{T}
we have

\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q}u\leq 4R\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}+C\frac{\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}}{R}\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}

for a constant
C=C(N,C_{1})
.

Proof..

Applying the Gluing Lemma
4.9
we obtain for a.e.
(x_{1},t_{1}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in Q
that

\displaystyle|u(x_{1},t_{1})

\displaystyle-u(x_{2},t_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{R}}\underbrace{|u(x_{1},t_{1})-u(x,t_{1})|}_{\leq 2R\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}}\eta_{R}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{B_{R}}\underbrace{|u(x,t_{2})-u(x_{2},t_{2})|}_{\leq 2R\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}}\eta_{R}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\phantom{=\,}+\bigg|\int_{B_{R}}[u(x,t_{1})-u(x,t_{2})]\eta_{R}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 4R\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}+C\frac{\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}}{R}\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}.

This proves the asserted oscillation estimate.
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
1<p\leq 2
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, and
u
be a weak solution to the parabolic system (
1.1
) with (
1.2
)
1
in the sense of Definition
2.6
. Assume that
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(E_{T})
. Then, for every cylinder
Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\Subset E_{T}
we have

\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u\leq CR\Big(\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}+\mu+\lambda\Big)

with
C=C(N,C_{1})
.

Proof..

We apply the oscillation Lemma
4.10
with

Q=Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
. Consequently for a.e.
(x_{1},t_{1}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
we have

\displaystyle|u(x_{1},t_{1})

\displaystyle-u(x_{2},t_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq 4R\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}+C\lambda^{2-p}R\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}.

To estimate the second term on the right-hand side, we distinguish two cases. First, let
\lambda<\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}
be satisfied.
Then, we have

\displaystyle|u(x_{1},t_{1})-u(x_{2},t_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq 4R\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}+CR\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CR\Big(\mu+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}\Big).

In the other case, i.e. when
\lambda\geq\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}
holds, we get

\displaystyle|u(x_{1},t_{1})-u(x_{2},t_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq CR\Big(\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}+\lambda\Big).

Merging both cases yields the result.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

In the course of the proof of the Schauder estimates, we always assume

A\lambda\geq\mu
for some
A\geq 1
. Therefore, we can limit ourselves to state the estimate
in this case, i.e.

\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u\leq CR\Big(\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}+\lambda\Big)

with
C=C(N,C_{1},A)
.

5.
Schauder-type estimates

In this section we prove Schauder estimates for bounded weak solutions to the parabolic
p
-Laplace system (
1.1
) with Hölder continuous coefficients
a(x,t)
satisfying assumptions (
1.2
) with
\mu\in(0,1]
. We restrict our considerations
to
bounded
weak solutions

u\in L^{\infty}(E_{T},\mathbb{R}^{k})\cap L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap C\big([0,T];L^{2}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})\big).

For the moment, we assume additionally that

(5.1)

|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E_{T}).

Assumption (
5.1
) will be removed in Section
5.5
.

The
general geometric setup
is as follows. With
\varrho:=\tfrac{1}{4}\min\{1,\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{par}}(\mathsf{K},\partial_{\mathrm{par}}E_{T})\}
and
\tilde{z}\in\mathsf{K}
, we consider standard cylinders

(5.2)

Q_{\varrho}(\tilde{z})\subset Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})\subset Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})\subset Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})\Subset E_{T}

with
\varrho\leq R_{1}<R_{2}\leq 2\varrho\leq 1
and a parameter
\lambda\geq\frac{\mu}{A}
such that

(5.3)

\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq A\lambda,

where the number
A\geq 1
will be chosen in terms of given data.
With these quantities we define

(5.4)

R_{o}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\min\big\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big\}(R_{2}-R_{1}).

Then, for any
z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
and any
R\leq 2R_{o}
the intrinsic cylinders
Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
are contained in
Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})
.
For a parameter
\kappa\in(0,1)
to be specified later, and a radius

r\in(0,\frac{1}{8}R_{o})
we define

(5.5)

R:=\Big(\frac{8r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\kappa}R_{o}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad r=\tfrac{1}{8}\Big(\frac{R}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}-1}R.

With this particular specification of
R
we note that
R<R_{o}
and
r<\frac{1}{8}R
. Moreover, we have

(5.6)

\frac{R}{R_{o}}=8^{\kappa}\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\kappa}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{R}{r}=8^{\kappa}\Big(\frac{R_{o}}{r}\Big)^{1-\kappa}.

The choice of the intermediate radius
R
together with the selection of
\kappa
facilitates an interpolation argument in Section
5.2
in order to derive a Campanato-type estimate.

5.1.
Freezing coefficients

Let
z_{o}=(x_{o},t_{o})\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{8}R_{o})
and
R
defined according to (
5.5
). In the following we consider the unique weak solution
w

to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem

(5.7)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\partial_{t}w-\Div\Big(a(x_{o},t)\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dw\Big)=0&\mbox{in $Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})$,}\\[6.0pt]
w=u&\mbox{on $\partial_{\mathrm{par}}Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})$,}\end{array}\right.

where
\mu\in[0,1]
, in the sense of Definition
2.5
.

In view of the
C^{1,\alpha}
-regularity theory by DiBenedetto and Friedman
[
16
,
18
,
19
,
20
]
the auxiliary function
w
satisfies good
a priori
estimates which we adapt and present next. Essentially, the result states that if the inclusion
Q_{2\mathfrak{R}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\Subset Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
and the intrinsic relation (
5.8
) hold, then the Campanato-type estimate (
5.9
) holds.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
p>1
,
\mu\in[0,1]
and
\mathfrak{A}\geq 1
, and suppose that
assumptions (
1.2
)
1
are satisfied.
There exist constants

C>0
and
\beta\in(0,1)
, depending
only on the data
N,p,C_{o},C_{1}
, and
\mathfrak{A}
, such that whenever
w

is a weak solution to the parabolic system (
5.7
)
1
in the sense of Definition
2.6
, if

Q_{2\mathfrak{R}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\Subset Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
with
\lambda>0
and

\mathfrak{R}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}R)

satisfies

(5.8)

\sup_{Q_{2\mathfrak{R}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\mathfrak{A}\lambda,

then we have

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{\mathfrak{R}}\Big)^{\beta p}\lambda_{\mu}^{p}\qquad\mbox{for all $\tau\in(0,\mathfrak{R}]$},

where
\lambda_{\mu}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{A}^{2}\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}}
, and
(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}
is the integral average of
Dw
on

Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
.

Proof..

In view of (
5.8
) we may assume
\lambda>\frac{\mu}{\mathfrak{A}}
, since otherwise
Dw\equiv 0
on
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
and the result is trivial.
In the following we abbreviate
b(t):=a(x_{o},t)
. We introduce the auxiliary function

v:=\mathfrak{A}^{-1}w\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tilde{\mu}:=\mathfrak{A}^{-1}\mu.

Then,
v
is a weak solution to the parabolic system

\partial_{t}v=\Div\Big(b(t)\mathfrak{A}^{p-2}\big(\tilde{\mu}^{2}+|Dv|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Dv\Big)=0\quad\mbox{in $E_{T}$.}

Hence,
[
11
, Theorem 1.3]
is applicable to
v
after replacing
C_{o},C_{1}
by
\mathfrak{A}^{p-2}C_{o},\mathfrak{A}^{p-2}C_{1}
and
\mu
by
\tilde{\mu}
.
The proof of
[
11
, Theorem 1.3]
is presented in
[
11
, Section 5.2]
; the estimates are stated for cylinders of the form

\widetilde{Q}_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}):=B_{\tau}(x_{o})\times\big(t_{o}-(\tilde{\mu}^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{\frac{2-p}{2}}\tau^{2},t_{o}\big].

In particular, with
\lambda_{\tilde{\mu}}:=\sqrt{\lambda^{2}-\tilde{\mu}^{2}}>0
we have

(5.10)

\widetilde{Q}_{\tau}^{(\lambda_{\tilde{\mu}})}(z_{o})=Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})

for every
\tau\in(0,2\mathfrak{R}]
,
and assumption (
5.8
) can be rewritten in the form

\sup_{\widetilde{Q}_{2\mathfrak{R}}^{(\lambda_{\tilde{\mu}})}(z_{o})}|Dv|\leq\lambda_{\tilde{\mu}}.

This corresponds to the sup-estimate in
Step 1
on p. 27 of
[
11
]
.
Then, we can repeat the
arguments in
Steps 2 – 4
on pp. 27 – 29 and arrive at
[
11
, Eqn. (5.16)]
with
\lambda_{\tilde{\mu}}

in place of
\lambda
. Since the coefficients
b(t)
are independent of
x
, the upper bound
\varrho_{o}
on the radius
\mathfrak{R}
can be avoided in the present situation.
Therefore, with the Lebesgue representative
\Gamma_{z_{o}}
of
Du(z_{o})
,
we obtain the Campanato-type estimate

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{\widetilde{Q}_{\tau}^{(\lambda_{\tilde{\mu}})}(z_{o})}|Dv-\Gamma_{z_{o}}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{\mathfrak{R}}\Big)^{\beta p}\lambda_{\tilde{\mu}}^{p}\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{\mathfrak{R}}\Big)^{\beta p}\lambda^{p}

for every
\tau\in(0,\mathfrak{R}]
, with constants

C>0
and
\beta\in(0,1)
depending on

N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\mathfrak{A}
. In view of (
5.10
),
this implies

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Dv-(Dv)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C(p)\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Dv-\Gamma_{z_{o}}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{\mathfrak{R}}\Big)^{\beta p}\lambda^{p}.

Recalling that
w=\mathfrak{A}v
, this yields the claim (
5.9
).
∎

In the case
p=2
the intrinsic coupling (
5.8
) is superfluous, whereas the Campanato-type estimate (
5.9
) improves to be

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}(z_{o})}\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{\mathfrak{R}}\Big)^{2\beta}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\mathfrak{R}}(z_{o})}|Dw|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

The right-hand integral can be estimated by the integral of
|Du|^{2}
instead of
|Dw|^{2}
thanks to the energy inequality in Lemma
4.7
