, while the comparison estimate in the same lemma yields

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{\mathfrak{R}}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle|Du-Dw|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\mathfrak{R}^{\alpha p}\iint_{Q_{\mathfrak{R}}(z_{o})}|Du|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Then, it is not hard to combine them and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},\tau}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{\mathfrak{R}}\Big)^{2\beta}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\mathfrak{R}}(z_{o})}|Du|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\mathfrak{R}^{2\alpha}\iint_{Q_{\mathfrak{R}}(z_{o})}|Du|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Interpolating the radii
\tau
and
\mathfrak{R}
will give a Campanato-type estimate for
Du
.

Even though our program follows this spirit, yet the non-linearity of the system brings essential difficulties. In general, we cannot replace
\lambda_{\mu}
in (
5.9
) with integrals of
Dw
or
Du
.
The following proposition, however, gives an “interpolative” remedy for this difficulty.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
w
be the unique weak solution to the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem (
5.7
) in the sense of Definition
2.5
, and assume that (
5.3
) is satisfied for some
A\geq 1
. Then, there exist exponents
\theta_{2}=\theta_{2}(N,p)>0
and
\beta\in(0,1)
, and a constant
C>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and any
\tau\in(0,\tfrac{1}{8}R]
the Campanato-type estimate

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)^{\beta}\max\Bigg\{\varepsilon^{-\theta_{2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\,,\,\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\Bigg\},

holds true. The constants
\beta
and
C
depend only on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},A
and in the case
1<p<2
also on
k
.

Proof..

Prior to estimating
Dw
, we recall two comparison estimates to be used. In fact, Lemma
4.4
gives the oscillation estimate

(5.11)

\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}w\leq\sqrt{k}\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u=:\sqrt{k}\,\bm{\omega}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)},

whereas Lemma
4.7
gives the
energy inequality

(5.12)

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

with
C=C(p,C_{o},C_{1})
.

Next, we aim to bound
Dw
via the
L^{p}
-norm of
Du
interpolating with
\lambda
. The gradient bounds from Section
3
are at our disposal as
a(x_{o},t)
depends only on
t
.
Due to the different forms of quantitative
L^{\infty}
-gradient estimates we distinguish between two cases. In the
sub-quadratic case

1<p<2
, Corollary
3.9
and the oscillation estimate (
5.11
) yield

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle|Dw|

(5.13)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}}{\lambda R}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}}{\lambda R}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{N(2-p)+2p}{4p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\vee\varepsilon\lambda

for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, since
\lambda\geq\frac{\mu}{A}
as a consequence of (
5.3
).
The constant
C
depends only on
N,k,p,C_{o},C_{1}
and
A
, while
\theta
on
N
and
p
. Here,
k
enters
C
via (
5.11
).
Now we claim
\bm{\omega}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}\leq C\lambda R
.
Indeed, by (
5.3
) and
Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})
we have

A\lambda\geq\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore, from Remark
4.12
we infer

\bm{\omega}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}\leq C(N,C_{1},A)R\Big(\underbrace{\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}}_{\leq\,A\lambda}+\lambda\Big)\leq C(N,C_{1},A)R\lambda.

Using this as well as (
5.12
) in (
5.1
), we have shown in the case
1<p<2
that

(5.14)

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Dw|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\vee\varepsilon\lambda

for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
with constants
C=C(N,k,p,C_{o},C_{1},A)
and
\theta=\theta(N,p)>0
.

Next, we shall derive an similar estimate as (
5.14
) in the
super-quadratic case
p\geq 2
. For this purpose we apply Corollary
3.12
with
C_{2}=0
and
\sigma=\frac{1}{4}
on
Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
and then the energy estimate (
5.12
). In the resulting inequality we reduce the exponent of the energy integral of
u
from
\frac{1}{2}
to
\frac{1}{2p}
using condition (
5.3
). In this way we get

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Dw|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\lambda^{2-p}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\vee\varepsilon\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\lambda^{\frac{2-p}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\vee\varepsilon\lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{C\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\lambda^{\frac{1-p}{2}}\bigg[\underbrace{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}_{\leq\,(A\lambda)^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}\vee\varepsilon\lambda

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\vee\varepsilon\lambda,

for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, with a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},A)
and
\theta=\theta(N,p)>0
.

Inequalities (
5.14
) and (
5.1
) have the same form and they also play a central role in the following. First, we use them with
\varepsilon=1
together with condition (
5.3
) to ensure that
w
satisfies an intrinsic coupling on
Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Dw|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[\underbrace{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}_{\leq\,(A\lambda)^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\vee\lambda\leq C\lambda,

so that

(5.16)

\sup_{Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Dw|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\lambda.

Therefore, the requirement of the
a priori
estimate from
Proposition
5.1
is fulfilled for
w
on
Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
with
C
in place of
\mathfrak{A}
. Replacing

\mathfrak{R}
by
\frac{1}{8}R
in Proposition
5.1
, we obtain for any
0<\tau\leq\frac{1}{8}R

(5.17)

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)^{\beta p}\lambda^{p}

for some exponent
\beta=\beta(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},A)\in(0,1)
. In the sub-quadratic case
\beta
depends additionally on
k
, entering via
C
in (
5.16
). Let us further estimate (
5.17
) by the gradient bounds in (
5.14
), resp. (
5.1
):

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\|Dw\|^{p-1}_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\frac{1}{4}R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}\bigg[\underbrace{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}_{\leq C(\tau/R)^{\beta p}\lambda^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\frac{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\vee\varepsilon\lambda\Bigg]^{p-1}\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)^{\beta}\lambda

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)^{\beta}\Bigg[\frac{\lambda^{\frac{p+1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta(p-1)}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}\vee\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\Bigg].

Although this procedure reduces the exponent from
\beta p
to
\beta
, it interpolates between the energy of
u
and
\lambda
.

If the second entry in the maximum dominates the first one, the claimed Campanato-type estimate is obvious. Therefore, we can consider the other case:

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{\frac{p+1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta(p-1)}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}>\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p},

which is the same as

\displaystyle\lambda<\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2(\theta+1)}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}=:\frac{\mathsf{E}^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\varepsilon^{2(\theta+1)}},

where the meaning of
\mathsf{E}
is obvious.
Use this bound of
\lambda
to estimate the first entry by

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{\frac{p+1}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta(p-1)}}

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}

\displaystyle<\bigg[\frac{\mathsf{E}^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\varepsilon^{2(\theta+1)}}\bigg]^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{E}^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}}{\varepsilon^{\theta(p-1)}}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2\theta p+p+1}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Joining the two cases we obtain for any
\tau\in(0,\tfrac{1}{8}R]
that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\tau}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},\tau}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)^{\beta}\max\Bigg\{\varepsilon^{-\theta_{2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\,,\,\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\Bigg\},

where
\theta_{2}(N,p):=2\theta p+p+1
.
∎

5.2.
A Campanato-type estimate

Having the Campanato-type estimate for
Dw
at hand, the goal of this section is to derive a Campanato-type estimate for
Du
. In order for that, recall
r\in(0,\frac{1}{8}R_{o})
and the intermediate radius
R
so-defined in (
5.5
). First we write down the mean oscillation of
Du
and use the quasi-minimality of the mean value to estimate. Then we invoke the comparison estimate from Lemma
4.7
, that is,

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\,R^{\alpha_{\ast}p}\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where
\alpha_{\ast}
is defined in (
4.8
),
as well as the Campanato-type estimate from Proposition
5.2
for
Dw
. As such we obtain a preliminary version as follows:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C(p)\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Dw)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(\frac{R}{r}\Big)^{N+2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du-Dw|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C(p)\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Dw-(Dw)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{R}{r}\Big)^{N+2}R^{\alpha_{\ast}p}\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}+C\Big(\frac{r}{R}\Big)^{\beta}\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\theta_{2}}\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}\,,\,\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\Big\}

(5.18)

\displaystyle=:\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II},

where we abbreviated

\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Note that the Campanato-type estimate from Proposition
5.2
is applicable since
r<\frac{1}{8}R
by the choice of
R
in (
5.5
). The constant
C
in the term
\mathbf{I}
depends only on
p,C_{o},C_{1}
, while the one in
\mathbf{II}
depends additionally on
N
and
A
, and in the sub-quadratic case also on
k
. The Hölder exponent
\alpha_{\ast}
was defined in (
4.8
), while
\beta
is the Hölder exponent from (
5.17
).

The radius
R
so-defined in (
5.5
) is intermediate. To proceed further we need to estimate the integral

\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}
in terms of

\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}
. If we simply enlarge the domain from
Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
to
Q_{R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
in the integral, we would get a factor
(R_{o}/R)^{N+2}\geq 1
whose exponent is too large to be absorbed later.
For this reason we perform an interpolation argument and split

\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}=\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{1-\delta}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta},

with some
\delta\in(0,1)
to be chosen later, and estimate the first term with the help of condition (
5.3
).
Thereby we take advantage of the fact that
Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
is contained in
Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})
. In the second term, we enlarge the domain of integration. In this way we get

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq(A\lambda)^{p(1-\delta)}\Big(\frac{R_{o}}{R}\Big)^{\delta(N+2)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}.

Assuming that
\alpha_{\ast}p-\delta(N+2)\geq 0
, and using (
5.6
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{R}{r}\Big)^{N+2}\Big(\frac{R_{o}}{R}\Big)^{\delta(N+2)}R^{\alpha_{\ast}p}(A\lambda)^{p(1-\delta)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}

\displaystyle=C\Big(\frac{R}{r}\Big)^{N+2}\Big(\frac{R}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{\ast}p-\delta(N+2)}R_{o}^{\alpha_{\ast}p}(A\lambda)^{p(1-\delta)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{\ast}p\kappa-(1+\kappa\delta-\kappa)(N+2)}R_{o}^{\alpha_{\ast}p}(A\lambda)^{p(1-\delta)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{\ast}p\kappa-(1+\kappa\delta-\kappa)(N+2)}(A\lambda)^{p(1-\delta)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}.

To obtain the last line we used
R_{o}\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, which holds true by the choice of
R_{o}
in (
5.4
).
For the estimate of
\mathbf{II}
we use
R\leq R_{o}
and again (
5.6
) to get

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R}\Big)^{\beta}\max\bigg\{\Big(\frac{R_{o}}{R}\Big)^{\delta(N+2)}\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\delta)}}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{2}}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta},\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\beta}\Big(\frac{R}{R_{o}}\Big)^{-\beta-\delta(N+2)}\max\bigg\{\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\delta)}}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{2}}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta},\underbrace{\Big(\frac{R}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\delta(N+2)}}_{\leq 1}\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\beta-\kappa\beta-\kappa\delta(N+2)}\max\bigg\{\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\delta)}}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{2}}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta},\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\bigg\}.

The choice of

\kappa:=\frac{N+2+\beta}{N+2+\beta+\alpha_{\ast}p}\in(0,1)

ensures that the exponents of
r/R_{o}
in the estimates for
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
coincide; the common exponent is

\frac{\alpha_{\ast}\beta p}{N+2+\beta+\alpha_{\ast}p}-\delta\frac{(N+2)(N+2+\beta)}{N+2+\beta+\alpha_{\ast}p}.

Therefore, it is natural to choose
\delta
in the form

\delta:=\frac{\alpha_{\ast}\beta p}{2(N+2)(N+2+\beta)}\in(0,1).

Note that the condition
\delta\leq\frac{\alpha_{\ast}p}{N+2}

is satisfied by this choice.
In this way, the exponents of
r/R_{o}
on the right-hand side of the estimates of
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
are equal to

\alpha_{o}p
, where

(5.19)

\alpha_{o}:=\frac{\alpha_{\ast}\beta}{2(N+2+\beta+\alpha_{\ast}p)}\in(0,1).

Now we use the estimates of
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
in (
5.2
) to obtain
for every
r\in(0,\frac{1}{8}R_{o})
that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\max\bigg\{\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\delta)}}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{2}}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta},\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\bigg\}

for a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},A)
. In the sub-quadratic case the constant
C
additionally depends on
k
. If the second entry in the maximum dominates the first, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}.

In the opposite case, i.e. if

\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\delta)}}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{2}}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}>\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\lambda<\frac{\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{\theta_{2}+p-1}{p\delta}}},

we obtain for the first entry in the maximum

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\delta)}}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{2}}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{2}}}\bigg[\frac{\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{\theta_{2}+p-1}{p\delta}}}\bigg]^{p(1-\delta)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\delta}\equiv\frac{\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}}{\varepsilon^{\theta_{3}}},

where
\theta_{3}:=\frac{\theta_{2}+(p-1)(1-\delta)}{\delta}
. Note that

\theta_{3}=\theta_{3}(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha)
. In the
sub-quadratic case
\theta_{3}
depends additionally on
k
. Joining the two cases we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\theta_{3}}\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)},\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\Big\}.

The preceding inequality holds for any center
z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
and for any radius
r\in(0,\frac{1}{8}R_{o})
. For radii
r\in[\frac{1}{8}R_{o},R_{o}]
it still holds as

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C(p)\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\Big(\frac{R_{o}}{r}\Big)^{N+2+\alpha_{o}p}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p)8^{N+2+\alpha_{o}p}\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(N,p)\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}.

Together the last two inequalities imply for any

z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
, any
r\in(0,R_{o}]
,
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
that

(5.20)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\theta_{3}}\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)},\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\Big\}.

Note that
C
depends on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},A
, while
\alpha_{o}\in(0,1)
and
\theta_{3}>0
additionally depend on
\alpha
. Moreover, in the sub-quadratic case
1<p<2
all constants depend also on
k
. Choosing
\varepsilon=1
in (
5.20
) we obtain for any
z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
the
Campanato-type estimate

(5.21)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\lambda^{p}\quad\forall\,r\in(0,R_{o}].

Here we exploited the fact that
Q_{R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})
and condition (
5.3
) in order to estimate the integral
\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}
in terms of
\lambda^{p}
.

5.3.
Quantitative local gradient bound

Based on the Campanato-type estimates (
5.20
) and (
5.21
) and still assuming that
|Du|
is locally bounded, in this section we show an explicit quantitative
L^{\infty}
-gradient estimate in terms of the oscillation.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
p>1
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, and
u
be a bounded weak solution to the parabolic system (
1.1
) with (
1.2
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
. Assume (
5.1
) holds true. Then there exists a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha,k)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\varrho}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})}u}{\varrho}+\Big(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})}u}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu\bigg],

for any
Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})\Subset E_{T}
with
2\varrho\leq 1
.

Proof..

The setup is the same as described in (
5.2
). In particular,
Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})\Subset E_{T}
denotes a standard cylinder with radius
2\varrho\leq 1
and
R_{1},R_{2}
are radii such that
\varrho\leq R_{1}<R_{2}\leq 2\varrho
.
Consider
z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
and
\lambda\geq\frac{\mu}{A}
satisfying (
5.3
) for some
A\geq 1
.
This choice allows us to apply the Campanato-type estimates (
5.20
) based on which we first show that the means
(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}
are convergent in the limit
r\downarrow 0
. To this aim we consider a dyadic sequence of radii
r_{i}:=2^{-i}R_{o}
, with
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and show that the means
(Du)_{z_{o},r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}
form a Cauchy sequence. Thus, let
j<\ell
be in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Using (
5.20
) with
r_{i}
for
i\in\{j,\dots,\ell-1\}
instead of
r
, we have

(5.22)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r_{i}}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\theta_{3}}\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)},\varepsilon^{p-1}\lambda^{p}\Big\},

so that

\displaystyle\big|(Du)_{z_{o},r_{\ell}}^{(\lambda)}-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{j}}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=j}^{\ell-1}\big|(Du)_{z_{o},r_{i+1}}^{(\lambda)}-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=j}^{\ell-1}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r_{i+1}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}\big|\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2^{N+2}\sum_{i=j}^{\ell-1}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}},\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda\Big\}\sum_{i=j}^{\ell-1}\Big(\frac{r_{i}}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{2^{-j\alpha_{o}}}{1-2^{-\alpha_{o}}}\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}},\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda\Big\}.

Recall that
C
depends on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},A
, while
\alpha_{o}\in(0,1)
and
\theta_{3}>0
additionally depend on
\alpha
.
Since
the right-hand side converges to
0
as
j\to\infty
, we conclude that the sequence of mean values
\big((Du)_{z_{o},r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}\big)_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is a Cauchy sequence. Therefore, the limit

\Gamma_{z_{o}}:=\lim_{i\to\infty}(Du)_{z_{o},r_{i}}^{(\lambda)}\in\mathbb{R}^{kN}

exists. This allows us to pass to the limit
\ell\to\infty
in (
5.3
) with the result

(5.24)

\displaystyle\big|\Gamma_{z_{o}}-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{j}}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{1-2^{-\alpha_{o}}}\Big(\frac{r_{j}}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}}\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}},\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda\Big\}.

The last inequality true for the sequence
r_{j}
actually admits a “continuous” version for all radii
r\in(0,R_{o}]
.
In fact, given
r\in(0,R_{o}]
, we choose
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
r_{j+1}<r\leq r_{j}
. In view of (
5.24
) and (
5.22
) we obtain

\displaystyle\big|\Gamma_{z_{o}}-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\Gamma_{z_{o}}-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{j}}^{(\lambda)}\big|+\big|(Du)_{z_{o},r_{j}}^{(\lambda)}-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\Gamma_{z_{o}}-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{j}}^{(\lambda)}\big|+2^{N+2}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r_{j}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r_{j}}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}

(5.25)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}}\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}},\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda\Big\}.

This shows

\Gamma_{z_{o}}=\lim_{r\downarrow 0}(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)},

which means that
\Gamma_{z_{o}}
is the Lebesgue representative of
Du
in
z_{o}
. Estimate (
5.3
) essentially yields the
C^{1,\alpha}
-regularity of
u
which will be elaborated in the next section. Here, we first exploit it to obtain the claimed gradient bound. Indeed, we choose

(5.26)

\lambda:=\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

so that (
5.3
) is satisfied with
A=1
.
Choosing
r=R_{o}
in (
5.3
), we get

\displaystyle\big|\Gamma_{z_{o}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|(Du)_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big|+\big|\Gamma_{z_{o}}-(Du)_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}},\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda\Big\}

(5.27)

\displaystyle\leq C\max\Big\{\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}},\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda\Big\}.

We estimate the integral on the right-hand side of (
5.3
) using the energy inequality from Proposition
4.1
in the form given in Remark
4.2

on
Q_{R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
with
\xi=(u)_{z_{o},2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}
. In this way we get

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Bigg[\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o},2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}}{(2R_{o})^{p}}+\lambda^{p-2}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o},2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{2}}{(2R_{o})^{2}}+\mu^{p}\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(5.28)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u}{2R_{o}}\bigg)^{p}+\lambda^{p-2}\bigg(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u}{2R_{o}}\bigg)^{2}+\mu^{p}\Bigg]

with
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1})
.

We now distinguish between the sub- and super-quadratic case. First consider the case
1<p<2
. Since
Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})
, the choice of
\lambda
from (
5.26
) yields

\lambda=\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\Big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This allows us to apply Remark
4.12
and conclude that

\displaystyle\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u

\displaystyle\leq 2CR_{o}\Big(\underbrace{\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o}))}}_{\leq\,\lambda}+\lambda\Big)\leq 4CR_{o}\lambda,

where
C=C(N,C_{1})
.
Using this inequality in (
5.3
) to bound the second term on the right-hand side yields

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u}{2R_{o}}\bigg)^{p}+\mu^{p}\Bigg].

In the super-quadratic case
p\geq 2
we observe by Young’s
inequality that

\displaystyle X^{p}+\lambda^{p-2}X^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\varepsilon}^{p-1}\lambda^{p}+2\tilde{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{2}(p-1)(p-2)}X^{p},

where we denoted
X=\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u/2R_{o}
for short and
\tilde{\varepsilon}\in(0,1]
. This observation in (
5.3
) yields

(5.29)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\varepsilon}^{p-1}\lambda^{p}+C\tilde{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{2}(p-1)(p-2)}\bigg(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u}{2R_{o}}\bigg)^{p}+C\mu^{p}.

Overall we have shown that (
5.29
)
holds true for any
p>1
with a constant
C
depending only on
N,p,C_{o}
, and
C_{1}
, and with
\frac{1}{2}(p-1)(p-2)
replaced by
\frac{1}{2}(p-1)(p-2)_{+}
.

Using this inequality in (
5.3
) we obtain

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{z_{o}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\Big(\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\Big)\lambda+\varepsilon^{-\frac{\theta_{3}}{p}}\bigg(\tilde{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{(p-1)(p-2)_{+}}{2p}}\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u}{2R_{o}}+\mu\bigg)\Bigg].

Choosing
\tilde{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{1+\frac{\theta_{3}}{p-1}}
we get

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{z_{o}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\bigg(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u}{2R_{o}}+\mu\bigg)\Bigg].

with a positive exponent
\theta_{4}
depending on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha
, and in the sub-quadratic case
p<2
also on
k
. The preceding inequality holds true for any
z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
. The oscillation term on the right-hand side can be estimated from above using the set inclusion
Q_{2R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})\subset Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})
. The latter applies because
R_{2}\leq 2\varrho
. Using the abbreviation
\bm{\omega}:=\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})}u
, and recalling that
\Gamma_{z_{o}}
is the Lebesgue representative of
Du
at
z_{o}

we have for almost every
z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
that

\displaystyle|Du(z_{o})|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{2R_{o}}+\mu\Big)\bigg].

Recalling the definition of
R_{o}
from (
5.4
) and passing to the supremum over
Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})
we get

(5.30)

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\big(1+\lambda^{\frac{2-p}{2}}\big)\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\mu\bigg].

Based the above estimate, we now perform an interpolation argument which differs slightly depending on
p
.
In the case
1<p<2
the exponent
\frac{2-p}{2}
is non-negative, so that by Young’s inequality with exponents
\frac{2}{2-p}
and
\frac{2}{p}
we can conclude

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\lambda^{\frac{2-p}{2}}\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{-\frac{2}{p}(\theta_{4}+\frac{(p-1)(2-p)}{2p})}}_{=:\,\varepsilon^{-\theta_{5}}}\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}.

Together with the choice of
\lambda
from (
5.26
) this leads to

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{5}}\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\mu\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\bigg[\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{5}}\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\mu\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|+C\bigg[\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{5}}\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\mu\bigg].

Here, we choose
C\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}=\frac{1}{2}
. This fixes
\varepsilon
in dependence on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},k
and yields for any
\varrho\leq R_{1}<R_{2}\leq 2\varrho
that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|+C\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu\bigg],

with a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha,k)
. At this point we can apply Lemma
2.2
and get the
quantitative local gradient bound

(5.31)

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\varrho}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\varrho}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu\bigg].

In the super-quadratic case
p\geq 2
the exponent
\frac{2-p}{2}
is negative. Therefore, we have to argue in a different way. Using (
5.26
) we first decrease the exponent in the sup-term from
1
to
\frac{2}{p}
, gaining a factor
\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}
. Subsequently, we apply (
5.30
). The additional power of
\lambda
then compensates the negative power
\lambda
in (
5.30
). The precise argument is as follows

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{p}}\Big(\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})}\big|Du\big|\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{p}}\bigg[\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lambda+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\big(1+\lambda^{\frac{2-p}{2}}\big)\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\varepsilon^{-\theta_{4}}\mu\bigg]^{\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\lambda+C\varepsilon^{-\frac{2\theta_{4}}{p}}\big(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{p}}+1\big)\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+C\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{p}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{2\theta_{4}}{p}}\mu^{\frac{2}{p}}.

This time we choose
\varepsilon
to satisfy
C\big(\varepsilon^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\big)^{\frac{2}{p}}=\frac{1}{4}
and obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\lambda+C\big(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{p}}+1\big)\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+C\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{p}}\mu^{\frac{2}{p}}.

Applying Young’s inequality with exponents
\frac{p}{p-2}
and
\frac{p}{2}
to those terms containing
\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{p}}
, we obtain for all radii
\varrho\leq R_{1}<R_{2}\leq 2\varrho
that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda+C\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})}|Du|+C\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu\bigg].

As in the sub-quadratic case, we recall the choice of
\lambda
from (
5.26
) and apply the iteration Lemma
2.2
which yields the gradient bound (
5.31
). In the super-quadratic case, the constant
C
depends on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1}
and
\alpha
. This completes the proof.
∎

The following result gives a version of the previous gradient estimate over a compact subset of
E_{T}
.

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
p>1
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, and
u
be a bounded weak solution to the parabolic system (
1.1
) with (
1.2
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
. Assume (
5.1
) holds true.
Then there exists a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha,k)
such that for any compact subset
\mathsf{K}\subset E_{T}

with
\varrho:=\tfrac{1}{4}\min\{1,\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{par}}(\mathsf{K},\partial_{\mathrm{par}}E_{T})\}
, we have
the quantitative local gradient bound

\sup_{\mathsf{K}}|Du|\leq C\bigg[\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}+\Big(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu\bigg].

Proof..

Recall that

\dist_{\mathrm{par}}(z_{1},z_{2}):=|x_{1}-x_{2}|+\sqrt{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}.

Then, for any
\tilde{z}\in\mathsf{K}
we have
Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})=B_{2\varrho}(\tilde{x})\times(\tilde{t}-(2\varrho)^{2},\tilde{t}]\Subset E_{T}
. Let

(5.32)

\mathsf{U}:=\bigcup_{\tilde{z}\in\mathsf{K}}Q_{\varrho}(\tilde{z}).

Then, the local gradient estimate from Proposition
5.3
applied on
Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})
implies that

\sup_{\mathsf{U}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\bigg[\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}+\Big(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu\bigg]

with
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha,k)
. Since
\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{U}
we get the asserted inequality.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

In the
super-critical case

p>\frac{2N}{N+2}
, we can
replace (
5.29
) by an estimate that is independent
of the oscillation of
u
. In fact, in view of the definition of

R_{o}
in (
5.4
) and
Q_{R}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R_{2}^{N+2}}{R_{o}^{N+2}\lambda^{2-p}}\underbrace{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}_{=:\,\mathsf{E}_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}}

(5.33)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{2R_{2}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{N+2}\max\Big\{\lambda^{p-2},\lambda^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\Big\}\mathsf{E}_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}.

By the choice of
\lambda
in (
5.26
), the integrand on the left-hand side is bounded from above by
\lambda^{p}
. In the case
\frac{2N}{N+2}<p<2
, we use this fact to estimate

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{p(1-d_{2})}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{d_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{2R_{2}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{d_{2}(N+2)}\max\Big\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p(2-p)(N+2)}{4}}\Big\}\big(\mathsf{E}_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}\big)^{d_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lambda^{p}+\Big(\frac{2R_{2}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{d_{2}(N+2)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}\big)^{d_{2}}+C_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{2R_{2}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{d_{1}(N+2)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}\big)^{d_{1}}

for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, where

d_{1}:=\frac{2p}{p(N+2)-2N}\quad\mbox{and}\quad d_{2}:=\tfrac{p}{2}

denote the
scaling deficits
.
For the last estimate, we applied Young’s inequality with exponents

\frac{4}{(2-p)(N+2)}
and
\frac{4}{p(N+2)-2N}=\frac{d_{1}}{d_{2}}
. Note that
C_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-\frac{(2-p)(N+2)}{p(N+2)-2N}}
.

For exponents in the super-quadratic range
p\geq 2
, we argue similarly.
In fact, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{p(1-d_{1})}\big(\mathsf{E}_{z_{o},R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{d_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{2R_{2}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{d_{1}(N+2)}\max\big\{\lambda^{p-2d_{1}},1\big\}\big(\mathsf{\mathsf{}}E_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}\big)^{d_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lambda^{p}+C_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{2R_{2}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{d_{2}(N+2)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}\big)^{d_{2}}+\Big(\frac{2R_{2}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{d_{1}(N+2)}\big(\mathsf{E}_{\tilde{z},R_{2}}\big)^{d_{1}},

for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.
In the last line, we applied Young’s inequality with exponents

\frac{p}{p-2d_{1}}
and
\frac{p}{2d_{1}}
. Here we have
C_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}(p-2)(N+2)}
.

Proceeding similarly as for the derivation of (
5.31
), but replacing (
5.29
) by the preceding inequality,
now leads to a gradient sup-bound that is independent of the oscillation of
u
.
More precisely, for every
p>\frac{2N}{N+2}
we obtain

\sup_{Q_{\varrho}(\tilde{z})}|Du|\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{2}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}(\tilde{z})}(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{d_{k}}{p}},

with a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha)
and the scaling deficits
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
.
For exponents in the sub-critical range
1<p\leq\frac{2N}{N+2}
, however, the above procedure is
not applicable, since then the exponent
\frac{N(2-p)}{2}
of
\lambda
that
appears in (
5.5
) is not smaller than

p
. Therefore, it seems to be unavoidable that the right-hand side
of estimate (
5.31
) depends on the oscillation of

u
in the sub-critical range.
\Box

5.4.
Hölder continuity of the gradient

Having established the quantitative
L^{\infty}
-gradient bound for locally bounded weak solutions to the parabolic system (
1.1
) under the qualitative assumption
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(E_{T})
, we will show in this section (under the same assumptions) that a quantitative gradient Hölder estimate also holds.
The main result is as follows.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
p>1
and
\mu\in(0,1]
. Then there is a constant
C
and a Hölder exponent
\alpha_{o}\in(0,1)
, both depending on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha
and
k
, so that whenever
u
is a bounded weak solution to the parabolic system (
1.1
) with (
1.2
) in the sense of Definition
2.6
, such that the assumption (
5.1
) holds true, then for any compact subset
\mathsf{K}\subset E_{T}
with
\varrho:=\tfrac{1}{4}\min\{1,\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{par}}(\mathsf{K},\partial_{\mathrm{par}}E_{T})\}
we have

\displaystyle|Du(x_{1},t_{1})-Du(x_{2},t_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\Bigg[\frac{|x_{1}-x_{2}|+\sqrt{\lambda^{p-2}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}}{\min\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\}\varrho}\Bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}

for any
(x_{1},t_{1}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in\mathsf{K}
, where

\lambda:=\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}+\Big(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu.

Proof..

Let

R_{1}:=\frac{1}{2}\varrho
,
R_{2}:=\varrho
, and
R_{o}:=\frac{1}{4}\min\big\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big\}\varrho
. We define the set
\mathsf{U}
as in (
5.32
).
Then, for any
\tilde{z}\in\mathsf{K}
the gradient bound from Corollary
5.4
and the definition of
\lambda
imply

\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z}))}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\big(\mu^{2}+\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{U})}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq A\lambda,

where
A=A(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},\alpha,k)
denotes the constant previously denoted by
C
in Corollary
5.4
.
This ensures that (
5.3
) holds on
Q_{R_{2}}(\tilde{z})
for any
\tilde{z}\in\mathsf{K}
. Therefore, the Campanato-type estimate (
5.21
) is available for any
z_{o}\in\mathsf{K}
and we obtain for every

0<r\leq R_{o}
that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\big|Du-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\lambda^{p}\quad\forall\,r\in(0,R_{o}].

In addition, (
5.3
) from the proof of Proposition
5.3
applies. Note that for the derivation of (
5.3
) we have chosen
\lambda
according to (
5.3
), which is also satisfied in our present setting.
With
\varepsilon=1
inequality (
5.3
) takes the form

\displaystyle\big|\Gamma_{z_{o}}-(Du)_{z_{o},r}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\max\Bigg\{\underbrace{\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{o}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}}_{\leq\,A\lambda}\,,\,\lambda\Bigg\}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\lambda^{p}.

Joining this inequality with the Campanato-type estimate for
u
from above, we obtain

(5.34)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle|Du-\Gamma_{z_{o}}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\lambda^{p}

for any
z_{o}\in\mathsf{K}
and any
r\in(0,R_{o}]
.
Now, consider
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathsf{K}
such that

0<\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})=|x_{1}-x_{2}|+\sqrt{\lambda^{p-2}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\varrho,

and let

r:=2\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})\quad\mbox{and}\quad z_{\ast}:=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(x_{1}+x_{2}),\min\{t_{1},t_{2}\}\big).

Then,
Q_{\frac{1}{2}r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{\ast})\subset Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{i})
for
i=1,2
. Assuming that
r\leq R_{o}
,
we can apply the decay estimate (
5.34
) on
Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1})
and
Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{2})
to conclude that

\displaystyle|Du(z_{1})

\displaystyle-Du(z_{2})|^{p}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\frac{1}{2}r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{\ast})}|\Gamma_{z_{1}}-\Gamma_{z_{2}}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(N,p)\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1})}|Du-\Gamma_{z_{1}}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{2})}|Du-\Gamma_{z_{2}}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}p}\lambda^{p}.

In the case
r>R_{o}
we have

\displaystyle|Du(z_{1})-Du(z_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\underbrace{\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{K})}}_{\leq\,A\lambda}\leq 2A\lambda\Big(\frac{r}{R_{o}}\Big)^{\alpha_{o}}.

The same inequality therefore holds true in both cases. If we also keep in mind the definitions of
r
and
R_{o}
we then get

\displaystyle|Du(z_{1})-Du(z_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\bigg[\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})}{\min\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\}\varrho}\bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}

for any
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathsf{K}
with
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}\varrho
. In cases the distance
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})

is larger than
\frac{1}{2}\varrho
, we again argue using the quantitative gradient estimate. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle|Du(z_{1})-Du(z_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\underbrace{\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{K})}}_{\leq\,A\lambda}\leq 2A\lambda\bigg[\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})}{\frac{1}{2}\varrho}\bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}

\displaystyle\leq 4A\lambda\bigg[\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})}{\min\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\}\varrho}\bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}.

All together, the last two inequalities prove the assertion.
∎

5.5.
Schauder estimates for parabolic

p
-Laplace systems

In the previous sections, we considered bounded weak solutions to the parabolic
p
-Laplace system (
1.1
) with Hölder continuous coefficients
a(x,t)
satisfying (
1.2
), assuming that
\mu>0
and the gradients are locally bounded.
Now, we shall remove the assumption that
|Du|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}
and also consider
\mu=0
.
To achieve this we consider smooth approximation
a_{i}(x,t)
of
a(x,t)
that retains the conditions (
1.2
) and generate approximating solutions
u_{i}
. Then we apply to
u_{i}
the
a priori
estimates from previous sections and manage to pass to the limit. In particular, we can cover the case
\mu=0
as these
a priori
estimates are independent of
\mu
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Let
\mathsf{K}\subset E_{T}
and
\varrho:=\tfrac{1}{4}\min\{1,\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{par}}(\mathsf{K},\partial_{\mathrm{par}}E_{T})\}
. Let
\varepsilon_{o}\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2}\varrho)
and define the inner parallel set with distance
\varepsilon_{o}
to
\partial E
by

\widetilde{E}:=\big\{x\in E\colon\dist(x,\partial E)>\varepsilon_{o}\big\}.

Choose a standard mollifier
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{1})
with
\int_{B_{1}}\phi\,\mathrm{d}x=1
. For a sequence
\varepsilon_{i}\in(0,\varepsilon_{o})
with
\varepsilon_{i}\downarrow 0
define the scaled mollifiers by
\phi_{\varepsilon_{i}}(x):=\varepsilon_{i}^{-N}\phi(\frac{x}{\varepsilon_{i}})
. The regularized coefficients
a_{i}
are defined by

a_{i}(x,t):=\int_{B_{\varepsilon_{i}}}a(x-y,t)\phi_{\varepsilon_{i}}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y

for every
x\in\widetilde{E}
and
t\in(0,T]
. Using the properties (
1.2
) of the coefficients
a
, it is not difficult to show that the following properties of
a_{i}
are valid:

(5.35)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}C_{o}\leq a_{i}(x,t)\leq C_{1},\\[6.0pt]
|a_{i}(x,t)-a_{i}(y,t)|\leq C_{1}|x-y|^{\alpha},\\[6.0pt]
|Da_{i}(x,t)|\leq c(N)C_{1}\varepsilon_{i}^{-1}\|D\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\\[6.0pt]
|a_{i}(x,t)-a(x,t)|\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{i}^{\alpha},\end{array}\right.

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
, for any
x,y\in\widetilde{E}
, and
t\in(0,T]
. Here,
C_{o},C_{1}
are the constants from (
1.2
). The regularization parameter
\mu_{i}
is defined by

\mu_{i}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\varepsilon_{i},&\mbox{for $\mu=0$,}\\[5.0pt]
\mu,&\mbox{for $\mu\in(0,1]$.}\end{array}\right.

With the coefficients
a_{i}
and the parameters
\mu_{i}
we denote by

u_{i}\in L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}(\widetilde{E},\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)\cap C\big([0,T];L^{2}(\widetilde{E},\mathbb{R}^{k})\big)

the unique weak solution to the regularized Cauchy-Dirichlet problem (see Definition
2.5
)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\partial_{t}u_{i}-\Div\Big(a_{i}(x,t)\big(\mu_{i}^{2}+|Du_{i}|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}Du_{i}\Big)=0,&\mbox{in $\widetilde{E}_{T}$,}\\[6.0pt]
u_{i}=u,&\mbox{on $\partial_{\mathrm{par}}\widetilde{E}_{T}$.}\end{array}\right.

To
u_{i}
and
u

we apply the comparison estimate from Lemmas
4.6
and
4.8
, respectively, with
a(x,t)
and
b(x,t):=a_{i}(x,t)
on
\widetilde{E}_{T}
. Then,
\mathsf{A}=\|a-a_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{E}_{T})}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{i}^{\alpha}
and therefore,

\displaystyle\iint_{\widetilde{E}_{T}}|Du-Du_{i}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\varepsilon_{i}^{\alpha p}+\varepsilon_{i}^{\frac{\alpha p}{p-1}}\Big)\iint_{\widetilde{E}_{T}}\big(\mu^{2}+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

if
\mu\in(0,1]
, while if
\mu=0
we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\widetilde{E}_{T}}|Du-Du_{i}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big((\varepsilon_{i}^{\alpha}+\varepsilon_{i})^{p}+(\varepsilon_{i}^{\alpha}+\varepsilon_{i})^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\Big)\iint_{\widetilde{E}_{T}}\big(1+|Du|^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

In both cases we have strong convergence of
Du_{i}
to
Du
, i.e.

Du_{i}\to Du\quad\mbox{in $L^{p}\big(\widetilde{E}_{T},\mathbb{R}^{k}\big)$ as $i\to\infty$.}

Next, the comparison principle from Lemma
4.4
yields
that
u_{i}\in L^{\infty}(\widetilde{E}_{T},\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
that

(5.36)

\displaystyle\bm{\omega}_{i}:=\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{\widetilde{E}_{T}}u_{i}\leq\sqrt{k}\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{\widetilde{E}_{T}}u\leq\sqrt{k}\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u=:\sqrt{k}\bm{\omega}.

Now, (
5.35
)
1
and (
5.35
)
3
ensure that the coefficients
a_{i}
satisfy the requirements of (
3.2
), i.e. they are trapped by
C_{o}
and
C_{1}
, and their gradient is bounded by
C_{2,i}:=c(N)C_{1}\varepsilon_{i}^{-1}\|D\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
By Propositions
3.4
and
3.10
we have that
|Du_{i}|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(\widetilde{E}_{T})
. However, to obtain quantitative gradient bounds we cannot rely on those results in Section
3
since
C_{2,i}\uparrow\infty
as
i\to\infty
. Instead, we apply the
a priori
estimates from Sections
5.3
and
5.4
. Such application is viable as
a_{i}
fulfill, thanks to (
5.35
)
1
and (
5.35
)
2
, the conditions (
1.2
), and moreover
|Du_{i}|\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(\widetilde{E}_{T})
. Consequently, Corollary
5.4
and Proposition
5.6
give that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathsf{K}}|Du_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\frac{\bm{\omega}_{i}}{\varrho}+\Big(\frac{\bm{\omega}_{i}}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu_{i}\bigg]=:C\lambda_{i}

and

(5.37)

\displaystyle|Du_{i}(z_{1})-Du_{i}(z_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{i}\Bigg[\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda_{i})}(z_{1},z_{2})}{\min\{1,\lambda_{i}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\}\varrho}\Bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}

for any subset
\mathsf{K}\subset\widetilde{E}_{T}
, and for any
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathsf{K}
. Note that we also used the simple fact
\tfrac{1}{4}\min\{1,\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{par}}(\mathsf{K},\partial_{\mathrm{par}}\widetilde{E}_{T})\}\geq\varrho-\varepsilon_{o}\geq\frac{1}{2}\varrho
. The constants
C
and
\alpha_{o}\in(0,1)
in the preceding estimates
depend only on
N
,
p
,
C_{o}
,
C_{1}
, and
\alpha
(and on
k
if
p<2
), and are in particular independent of
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
We abbreviate

\displaystyle\lambda:=\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}+\Big(\frac{\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{E_{T}}u}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}+\mu.

Then, (
5.36
) implies

(5.38)

\limsup_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}\leq\max\big\{k^{\frac{1}{2}},k^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\}\lambda=:\mathsf{k}\lambda\quad\forall\,i\in\mathbb{N},

so that

\limsup_{i\to\infty}\Big(\sup_{\mathsf{K}}|Du_{i}|\Big)\leq C\mathsf{k}\lambda.

Now, let
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathsf{K}
.
Note that
the right-hand side of (
5.37
) can be re-written as

(5.39)

C\bigg[\max\Big\{\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}},\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{2-p}{2}}\Big\}\frac{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}{\varrho}+\max\Big\{\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{p-2}{2}},\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}}\Big\}\frac{\sqrt{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}}{\varrho}\bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}.

All exponents of
\lambda_{i}
are non-negative. In fact, recalling the definition of

\alpha_{o}
from (
5.19
) we have
\alpha_{o}=\frac{\alpha_{\ast}\beta}{2(N+2+\beta+\alpha_{\ast}p)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{\ast}
. In view of the definition of

\alpha_{\ast}
from (
4.8
) this shows in the case
1<p\leq 2
that
\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{p-2}{2}\geq 2+\frac{p-2}{2}=\frac{p+2}{2}>0
and in the case
p\geq 2
that
\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{2-p}{2}\geq 2(p-1)+\frac{2-p}{2}\geq\frac{3p-2}{2}>0
.
This proves that the right-hand side of (
5.37
) is non-decreasing in
\lambda_{i}
. Moreover, in view of (
5.38
) we have for the first term in (
5.39
) that

\displaystyle\max\Big\{\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}},\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{2-p}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{(\mathsf{k}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}},(\mathsf{k}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{2-p}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathsf{k}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{2-p}{2}}\max\Big\{\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}},\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{2-p}{2}}\Big\},&\mbox{if $1<p<2$,}\\[6.0pt]
\mathsf{k}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}}\max\Big\{\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}},\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{2-p}{2}}\Big\},&\mbox{if $p\geq 2$,}\end{array}\right.

while the second one is estimated by

\displaystyle\max\Big\{\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{p-2}{2}},\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{(\mathsf{k}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{p-2}{2}},(\mathsf{k}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathsf{k}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}}\max\Big\{\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{p-2}{2}},\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}}\Big\},&\mbox{if $1<p<2$,}\\[6.0pt]
\mathsf{k}^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{p-2}{2}}\max\Big\{\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}+\frac{p-2}{2}},\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{o}}}\Big\},&\mbox{if $p\geq 2$.}\end{array}\right.

This leads to

\displaystyle\limsup_{i\to\infty}\big|Du_{i}(z_{1})-Du_{i}(z_{2})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\Bigg[\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})}{\min\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\}\varrho}\Bigg]^{\alpha_{o}},

for any
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathsf{K}
.
Therefore, the sequence
(Du_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
C^{\alpha_{o},\alpha_{o}/2}\big(\mathsf{K},\mathbb{R}^{Nk}\big)
. By the theorem of Ascoli-Arzelà there exists a uniformly converging subsequence, and since
Du_{i}\to Du
in
L^{p}\big(\mathsf{K},\mathbb{R}^{Nk}\big)
we get that
Du_{i}\to Du
uniformly in
\mathsf{K}
as
i\to\infty
. Passing to the limit we find that

\sup_{\mathsf{K}}|Du|\leq C\mathsf{k}\lambda\quad\mbox{and}\quad\big|Du(z_{1})-Du(z_{2})\big|\leq C\lambda\Bigg[\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{par}}^{(\lambda)}(z_{1},z_{2})}{\min\{1,\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\}\varrho}\Bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}

for any
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathsf{K}
. This finishes the proof of (
1.3
) and (
1.4
).
∎

6.
Applications to doubly non-linear equations

6.1.
Regularity for doubly non-linear parabolic equations

The main result of the present section deals with the gradient regularity of weak solutions
u:E_{T}\to\mathbb{R}

to doubly non-linear parabolic equations

(6.1)

\partial_{t}u^{q}-\Div\big(|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u\big)=0\quad\mbox{in $E_{T}$,}

in the
super-critical fast diffusion range
of
p
and
q
. The formulation of the result is based, among other things, on the pointwise value
u(x_{o},t_{o})>0
. For this reason, it is convenient to state the result for continuous weak solutions; see Remark
6.2
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Assume that
0<p-1<q<\frac{N(p-1)}{(N-p)_{+}}
.
There are constants
C,\widetilde{\bm{\gamma}}>1
and
\alpha_{o}\in(0,1)
, which depend only on
N
,
p
and
q
, such that if
u
is a
non-negative, continuous, weak solution to (
6.1
) in
E_{T}
,
if
u_{o}:=u(x_{o},t_{o})>0
, and if the set inclusion

\widetilde{{\bm{\gamma}}}Q_{o}:=K_{\widetilde{{\bm{\gamma}}}\varrho}(x_{o})\times\big(t_{o}-u_{o}^{q+1-p}(\widetilde{\bm{\gamma}}\varrho)^{p},t_{o}+u_{o}^{q+1-p}(\widetilde{\bm{\gamma}}\varrho)^{p}\big)\subset E_{T}

holds, then we have the gradient bound

(6.2)

\sup_{Q_{o}}|\nabla u|\leq\frac{Cu_{o}}{\varrho},

the Lipschitz estimate

(6.3)

|u(z_{1})-u(z_{2})|\leq Cu_{o}\,\Bigg[\frac{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}{\varrho}+\sqrt{\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}{u_{o}^{q+1-p}\varrho^{p}}}\,\Bigg],

and the gradient Hölder estimate

(6.4)

|\nabla u(z_{1})-\nabla u(z_{2})|\leq\frac{Cu_{o}}{\varrho}\,\Bigg[\,\frac{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}{\varrho}+\sqrt{\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}{u_{o}^{q+1-p}\varrho^{p}}}\,\Bigg]^{\alpha_{o}},

for all
z_{1}=(x_{1},t_{1}),z_{2}=(x_{2},t_{2})\in Q_{o}
.

Figure
1
illustrates the region where Theorem
6.1
holds.
We emphasize that Theorem
6.1
is false for
q=p-1
and
q=\frac{N(p-1)}{(N-p)_{+}}
; see Section
6.5
.

Figure 1.

Remark 6.2
.

In the statement of gradient regularity, the continuity of
u
is assumed in order to give an unambiguous meaning to
u(x_{o},t_{o})
. It suffices to state these estimates for the
upper semicontinuous regularization

u^{*}
of
u
, which is, for locally bounded solutions, uniquely determined and verifies
u=u^{*}
a.e. in
E_{T}
. See
[
34
, Theorem 2.3]
for details.

Based on Theorem
6.1
, the gradient estimates can also be formulated in a generic compact subset
\mathcal{K}
of
E_{T}
. In particular,
\nabla u
is Hölder continuous over
\mathcal{K}
, and precise estimates can be obtained in terms of
\sup_{\mathcal{K}}u
and
\dist(\mathcal{K},\partial E_{T})
.

Corollary 6.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
there exists
\alpha_{1}\in(0,1)
, depending only on
N,p
and
q
, such that the following holds. Let

\mathcal{K}\subset E\times(0,T)
be a compact subset and define

\varrho_{o}:=\inf_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{(x,t)\in\mathcal K,}{ (y,s)\in\partial E_T}}\big(|x-y|+|t-s|^{\frac{1}{p}}\big)

and
\mathcal{M}:=\max\{1,\sup_{\mathcal{K}}u\}
. Then we have the gradient
L^{\infty}
-estimate

(6.5)

\sup_{\mathcal{K}}|\nabla u|\leq C\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{q+1}{p}}}{\varrho_{o}},

as well as the gradient Hölder estimate

(6.6)

|\nabla u(x_{1},t_{1})-\nabla u(x_{2},t_{2})|\leq C\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{q+1}{p}}}{\varrho_{o}}\Bigg[\mathcal{M}^{\frac{q+1-p}{p}}\frac{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}{\varrho_{o}}+\sqrt{\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}{\varrho_{o}^{p}}}\,\Bigg]^{\alpha_{1}}

for every
(x_{1},t_{1}),

(x_{2},t_{2})\in\mathcal{K}
.

For
p=2
our result reduces to the case of the singular porous medium equation in the range
1<q<\frac{N}{(N-2)_{+}}
. With
q=\frac{1}{m}

and the substitution
w=u^{\frac{1}{m}}
this corresponds to the bound
\frac{(N-2)_{+}}{N}<m<1
for the standard form
\partial_{t}w-\Delta w^{m}=0
of the porous medium equation. Therefore, Theorem
6.1
recovers
as a special case the gradient estimates of DiBenedetto & Kwong & Vespri
[
21
, Theorem 2.1]
for weak solutions to the singular porous medium equation.

Our approach relies on a “time-insensitive” Harnack inequality established in
[
8
]
that is particular about this regime and the Schauder estimates for the parabolic
p
-Laplace equation developed here. We will give more details about the strategy in Section
6.3
.
The thrust of our contribution is to generate precise, quantitative, local estimates regarding weak solutions as well as their gradient that dictate their behavior. Moreover, it turns out that such estimates are sharp as evidenced by various explicit examples (see Section
6.5
).

6.2.
Notation and notion of solutions

By
K_{\varrho}(x_{o})

we denote the cube in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
with center
x_{o}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and side length
2\varrho>0
, whose faces are parallel to the coordinate planes in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
. When
x_{o}=0
we simply write
K_{\varrho}
, omitting the reference to the center. In this section, space-time cylinders are denoted by

Q_{R}(z_{o}):=K_{R}(x_{o})\times(t_{o}-R^{p},t_{o}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{o}):=K_{R}(x_{o})\times(t_{o}-R^{p},t_{o}+R^{p}).

The use of cubes instead of balls is brought by the Harnack inequality established in
[
8
]
.

Definition 6.4
.

A function
u

is termed a
weak super(sub)-solution
to the parabolic equation (
6.1
) in
E_{T}
, if

u\in C\big([0,T];L^{q+1}(E)\big)\cap L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}(E)\big)

and if the integral identity

(6.7)

\iint_{E_{T}}\Big[-|u|^{q-1}u\partial_{t}\zeta+|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u\cdot\nabla\zeta\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\geq(\leq)0

is satisfied for all non-negative test functions

(6.8)

\zeta\in W_{0}^{1,q+1}\big(0,T;L^{q+1}(E)\big)\cap L^{p}\big(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(E)\big).

A function that is both a weak super- and sub-solution is called a
weak solution
.

Remark 6.5
.

The function space (
6.8
) of
\zeta
guarantees the convergence of the integral in (
6.7
).

Remark 6.6
.

A notion of
local solution
is commonly used in the literature, cf.
[
17
,
7
,
9
]
.
We stress that this notion makes no essential difference from Definition
6.4
modulo a localization.

6.3.
Strategy

A few words about the proof and the underlying ideas are in order here.
The first observation is general in nature, and reflects a common principle in the treatment of non-linear parabolic equations with degeneracy as in (
6.1
). One crucial building block in the study of the problem is the choice of the correct geometry in space and time: only the proper choice of geometry will lead to homogeneous estimates. In the present situation it turns out that the natural cylinders for our purposes are given by
Q_{o}:=K_{\varrho}(x_{o})\times\big(t_{o}-u_{o}^{q+1-p}\varrho^{p},t_{o}+u_{o}^{q+1-p}\varrho^{p}\big)
, provided
u_{o}>0
. The geometry reflected by such cylinders is
intrinsic
as it varies according to the value of the solution at the center. Once the correct

intrinsic geometry

is identified, one can turn to the actual regularity assertions.

The starting point is the fact that, in the described parameter range a Harnack inequality without waiting-time phenomenon holds true. Therefore, from the assumption
u_{o}>0
we may conclude that
u
is controlled from above and below in terms of
u_{o}
on the symmetric, intrinsic, space-time cylinder
Q_{o}
(backward-forward cylinder).
More precisely, if
{\bm{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{h}}
denotes the Harnack constant, then
{\bm{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{h}}^{-1}\leq u/u_{o}\leq{\bm{\gamma}}_{\textrm{h}}
on
Q_{o}
.
Rescaling
u/u_{o}
in time and space to
the symmetric unit cylinder
\mathcal{Q}_{1}:=K_{1}(0)\times(-1,1)
leads to a weak solution
\tilde{u}
to the doubly non-linear parabolic equation (
6.1
) in
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
with values in
[{\bm{\gamma}}_{\textrm{h}}^{-1},{\bm{\gamma}}_{\textrm{h}}]
. This allows us to pass from
\tilde{u}
to
v:=\tilde{u}^{q}
. Then
v(y,s)
is a weak solution to the parabolic equation

\partial_{s}v-\Div\Big(\big(\tfrac{1}{q}\big)^{p-1}\tilde{u}^{(p-1)(1-q)}|\nabla v|^{p-2}\nabla v\Big)=0\quad\mbox{in $\mathcal{Q}_{1}$,}

with values in the interval
[{\bm{\gamma}}_{\textrm{h}}^{-q},{\bm{\gamma}}_{\textrm{h}}^{q}]
. Furthermore, denoting

\displaystyle a(y,s):=\big(\tfrac{1}{q}\big)^{p-1}\tilde{u}(y,s)^{(p-1)(1-q)},

the above equation can be interpreted as a parabolic equation of
p
-Laplace-type with measurable and bounded coefficients that are also uniformly bounded away from zero. This allows us to apply
the by now classical
C^{0,\alpha}
-regularity results from
[
17
]
to deduce that
v
is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous in
\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Q}_{1}
with some Hölder exponent
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Together with the lower and upper bound for
v
, such a fact implies that the coefficient
a
is also
\alpha
-Hölder continuous. In particular, this shows that
v
is a weak solution to a parabolic equation of
p
-Laplace-type with Hölder continuous coefficients in
\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Q}_{1}
. This reduces questions about Lipschitz and gradient Hölder regularity of
v
to whether or not corresponding Schauder estimates for weak solutions can be established. Since the exponent
p
can take values close to
1
in the described parameter range, boundedness of weak solutions must be assumed in the context of Schauder estimates. However, in the range of
p
and
q
required, this is indeed the case. Theorem
1.1

ensures
\alpha_{o}
-Hölder continuity of
Dv
for some Hölder exponent
\alpha_{o}\in(0,1)
together with the quantitative gradient bound

\sup_{\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|\nabla v|\leq C,

and the quantitative
\alpha_{o}
-Hölder gradient estimate

\big|\nabla v(y_{1},s_{1})-\nabla v(y_{2},s_{2})\big|\leq C\Big[|y_{1}-y_{2}|+\sqrt{|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\Big]^{\alpha_{o}}

for any
(y_{1},s_{1}),(y_{2},s_{2})\in\tfrac{1}{4}\mathcal{Q}_{1}
.
The asserted inequalities for the original solution
u
are obtained by rescaling
v
to
u
.

To summarize, the proof strategy consists of three major steps. In the first step, we prove that weak solutions are bounded in the parameter range under consideration. In the second step we show that for non-negative weak solutions an elliptic Harnack-type inequality holds. This means that the Harnack inequality holds without waiting-time (backward-forward Harnack inequality). Thus, in a neighborhood near a point
(x_{o},t_{o})
with
u_{o}=u(x_{o},t_{o})>0
weak solutions are controlled from below and from above by
u_{o}
.
In the third and last step, one substitutes
v=u^{q}
. The resulting equation of
v
can be interpreted as a parabolic
p
-Laplace equation with measurable coefficients, so that the classical result about Hölder regularity of weak solutions can be directly applied to it, and consequently the coefficients (essentially power functions of the solution) are identified as being themselves Hölder continuous. At this point the desired gradient regularity can be deduced from suitable Schauder estimates for parabolic
p
-Laplace equations with Hölder continuous coefficients. The peculiarity (and new feature) in this step is that we need to establish these Schauder estimates for any
p>1
. In particular,
p
may be close to
1
.
Needless to say, Schauder estimates for
p
close to 1, can only be true under the additional assumption that weak solutions are bounded, which, however, is granted for our application here.

A unique feature of weak solutions to (
6.1
) in the fast diffusion regime is that they might become extinct abruptly.
As a direct application of the gradient estimates from Theorem
6.1
and the integral Harnack inequality from
[
8
, Theorem 3.13]
, decay estimates for weak solutions at their extinction time can be established.

Corollary 6.7
.

Let
p
and
q
be as in Theorem
6.1
. Then there exist
constants
C>1
and
{\bm{\gamma}}_{o}>1
depending only on
N
,
p
and
q
, such that if
u
is a non-negative weak solution
to the doubly non-linear parabolic equation (
6.1
) in
E_{T}
and if
T
is an extinction time for
u
, i.e.
u(\cdot,T)=0
a.e. in
E
,
then, for any
x_{o}\in E
and
t_{o}\in(\tfrac{1}{2}T,T)
we have

\displaystyle u(x_{o},t_{o})

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\frac{T-t_{o}}{d^{p}(x_{o})}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q+1-p}},

\displaystyle\big|\nabla u(x_{o},t_{o})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d(x_{o})}\bigg[\frac{T-t_{o}}{d^{p}(x_{o})}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q+1-p}}.

Here,
d(x_{o}):=\dist(x_{o},\partial E)
denotes the usual Euclidean distance of
x_{o}
to the boundary
\partial E
. Moreover, for any
r\in(0,d(x_{o})/{\bm{\gamma}}_{o})

we have the following decay estimates for the oscillation of
u
and its gradient, i.e.

\displaystyle\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{K_{r}(x_{o})}u(\cdot,t_{o})

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{r}{d(x_{o})}\bigg[\frac{T-t_{o}}{d^{p}(x_{o})}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q+1-p}},

\displaystyle\operatornamewithlimits{osc}_{K_{r}(x_{o})}\nabla u(\cdot,t_{o})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d(x_{o})}\bigg[\frac{r}{d(x_{o})}\bigg]^{\alpha_{o}}\bigg[\frac{T-t_{o}}{d^{p}(x_{o})}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q+1-p}}.

Here,
\alpha_{o}\in(0,1)
is the Hölder exponent from Theorem
6.1
.

Corollary
6.7
will be proved in Section
6.7
, where we will also provide an estimate for the extinction time of the solution to a Cauchy-Dirichlet problem.

To prove Theorem
6.1
we will take advantage of the Schauder estimates established in this manuscript, and the “time-insensitive” Harnack inequality from
[
8
]
. However, before giving the proof, we recall some relevant results from the literature, and then briefly describe the optimality of the range of exponents
0<p-1<q<\frac{N(p-1)}{(N-p)_{+}}
considered in Theorem
6.1
.

6.4.
Remarks on the literature

The investigation of regularity of weak solutions was initiated by Ivanov in a series of papers. Local boundedness of weak solutions has been studied in
[
27
]
. Regarding this topic, we refer to
[
8
]
for a more detailed exposition. The issue of Hölder continuity of weak solutions requires more delicate analysis and have attracted a number of authors. The well-studied ranges are the borderline case
p-1=q
, the doubly singular case, i.e.
p-1<q
and
1<p<2
, as well as the doubly degenerate case, i.e.
q<p-1
and
p>2
. See
[
25
,
26
,
28
,
38
,
41
,
33
,
7
,
9
,
35
,
42
]
, and also Figure
2
